## 2026-08-04

### Fully exposed end-of-iteration Scatter

- Added opt-in `MEGATRON_NONUNIFORM_EP_END_ITERATION_SCATTER=1`, mutually exclusive with the A2A-aware Scatter scheduler. Gather and device-ordered EDP still launch during backward, but no Scatter collective or Scatter-specific host rendezvous is submitted there. Owner results are staged before reusable Gather slots are released, then every Scatter is submitted in canonical layer/group/owner/chunk order from `finish_grad_sync` after a global backward-complete CUDA fence.
- Fixed two correctness issues found during staged validation: continued split-host batches initially entered the legacy host barrier, and materializing all deferred layers at once reused packed Scatter buffers. The final implementation uses the device-ordered path for every split-host batch and materializes/drains one layer at a time.
- Focused unit tests passed (`5 passed, 112 deselected`). EP12/EP8 distributed correctness job `2579372` completed with exact full/reduced checksum agreement and zero rank spread.
- EP16 A/B profile job `2579701` completed on GB200 using the 14-stage `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E` workload. Owner-rank steady timing (iterations 3-12) was healthy `551.910 +/- 5.465 ms` versus NEP `681.680 +/- 2.373 ms`: `129.770 ms` or `23.513%` slowdown, `80.963%` parity.
- Rank 0's NEP trace contains no legacy `nep_split_wait_scatter_launch` markers. Deferred Scatter submission occurs in the final drain and has zero useful-compute overlap by construction. Scatter NCCL kernel union is about `16.071 ms`; the larger end-to-end gap also contains serialized staging/copyback and host/GPU idle in the final drain.
- Artifacts: `slurm_runs/lyris/coreai_comparch_sysarch-nep.ep16-end-scatter-profile-gb200-2579701.out` and `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep16_ep12_end_scatter_profile_gb200/`.

# NEP Approach A Journal

Append a dated entry whenever we do something new: code changes, job submissions, benchmark results, trace analysis, or decisions that change the next step. Keep entries factual and include job IDs, run dirs, and commits when available.

## 2026-08-03 - EP12/EP8 slot-lifetime fix and EP32 same-bucket benchmark

- EP12/EP8 diagnostics showed Gather plus EDP were exact across ranks before Scatter, localizing the
  corruption to deferred Scatter/copyback. The async owner-buffer window reused slots while deferred
  local-owner copies still referenced them. The fix records a CUDA completion event per reused slot,
  completes local-owner copies without changing scheduler cardinality, and waits on those events before
  slot reuse.
- Intermediate-scale job `2567647` passed 32 focused preflight tests and both EP12/EP8 distributed
  cases. Every gradient checksum field matched exactly and the wrapper reported
  `[ep12-8-slotfix5-split-correctness] PASS`.
- For a controlled EP32 comparison, retained `DDP_NUM_BUCKETS=16` and changed NEP from three to seven
  expert EDP groups. Healthy and NEP therefore both use four dense plus seven expert all-reduce buckets
  (11 total). Enabled bucket-ready Gather and immediate native EDP enqueue for NEP.
- Same-allocation GB200 job `2567828` completed healthy EP32/EP32 and NEP EP32/EP28 timing cases on 16
  contiguous, segment-4 nodes. Warmup-excluded iterations 3-10 were `553.300 +/- 4.081 ms` healthy and
  `597.137 +/- 2.138 ms` NEP. Owner-rank parity was `92.659%`; NEP overhead was `43.837 ms` or `7.923%`.
  Logs are under `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep32_ep28_same_buckets_gb200/timing`.

## 2026-08-03 - Bucket-ready Gather and immediate native EDP enqueue

  bucket now marks its dispatch boundary ready directly from native parameter `AccumulateGrad`
  counts, instead of waiting for the `BaseMoELayer` full-backward hook.

  Distributed EP8/EP4 job `2565391` then completed both stable and bucket-ready split cases; all
  12-rank gradient checksum fields matched exactly and the wrapper reported `PASS`.
- Added opt-in `MEGATRON_NONUNIFORM_EP_IMMEDIATE_EDP_ENQUEUE`. The split scheduler records the
  Gather completion event, queues the existing device-readiness wait, and immediately invokes
  native Megatron DDP `start_grad_sync()` on the same dispatch stream. Its host worker waits for
  Gather device completion and the EDP Gloo rendezvous before releasing the queued stream wait.
  Scatter remains deferred and ordered after the native EDP work handle.
- Fix-2 preflight job `2565894` passed 32 focused tests, including deferred and immediate EDP
  variants, plus Black, isort, and compile checks. Distributed GB300 job `2566135` completed stable
  and immediate-EDP EP8/EP4 cases on `theia[0114-0115,0125]`; all gradient checksum fields matched
  exactly (`relative_delta=0`) and the wrapper reported `PASS`. Debug output showed the EDP stream
  wait and native DDP submission before the readiness worker completed its Gather event wait.

## 2026-08-03 - Root cause of three-bucket NEP EDP overlap loss

- The corrected job `2563690` loses EDP hidden time almost entirely in bucket 2. Healthy per-bucket
  hidden time is `6.210`, `6.160`, and `4.767 ms`; parallel NEP is `5.864`, `5.859`, and
  `0.000 ms`. Bucket 2 therefore explains `4.767 ms` of the total `5.413 ms` reduction.
- Across owner ranks, healthy submits bucket-2 EDP at `233.473 ms` and its kernel starts at
  `233.556 ms` from backward start. NEP records Gather at `245.058 ms`, its kernel starts at
  `257.429 ms`, Gather ends at `258.361 ms`, and EDP is not submitted until `273.335 ms`.
- The code path explains both delays. NEP requires the `BaseMoELayer` full-backward boundary before
  `_launch_nep_dispatch_boundary_tasks`, while native DDP launches from parameter `AccumulateGrad`
  readiness. Its Gather stream then waits on the full-boundary `compute_ready_event`, placing the
  final Gather after the final model-EP work instead of at native bucket readiness.
- Split host phases do not submit EDP when Gather completes. They are progressed only when
  `_wait_for_nep_dispatch_launch` runs at a later MoE pre-hook or final drain. For bucket 2 this
  leaves `14.974 ms` on average between Gather completion and EDP host submission; the subsequent
  record-to-kernel gap is only `0.093 ms`, proving that this part is host scheduling, not NCCL skew.
- Replaying the same NEP EDP durations at healthy launch offsets increases modeled hidden time from
  `11.724` to `15.696 ms`, recovering `3.972 ms` (`73.4%`) of the hidden-time deficit. The primary
  cause is therefore late NEP readiness/submission, not lack of useful backward work.
- A complete fix must launch Gather from bucket parameter readiness and progress EDP as soon as its
  Gather dependency is satisfied. Progressing EDP earlier without moving Gather earlier can recover
  little because the final Gather itself is already behind the useful overlap window.

  three times `239,468,544` elements (`718,405,632` total), because an EP12 owner holds 12 experts

## 2026-08-03 - Bounded parallel owner-Gather submission

- Added an opt-in `MEGATRON_NONUNIFORM_EP_PARALLEL_GATHER_WINDOW`; its default value of `1`
  preserves the existing single ordered dispatch stream. A value of `2` starts at most two
  independent owner waves on separate CUDA streams and joins their completion events before the
  direct-Scatter completion boundary. Focused container validation passed Black, isort, Python
  compilation, and `30` tests (`88` deselected).
- EP8/EP4 job `2562618` passed exact gradient checksums, but its Gather process groups are disjoint
  pairs and form only one owner wave, so it could not test this change. Its serial/parallel timing
  difference was treated as allocation drift rather than evidence.
- EP16/EP12 GB200 job `2562746` completed `0:0` on contiguous nodes `lyris[0217-0224]`. This is the
  smallest tested topology with three overlapping owner waves (`[0,12]`, `[4,12]`, `[8,12]`). The
  exact two-iteration gradient checksum gate matched all fields with zero relative delta.
- Same-allocation timing, iterations 4-10: serial `580.500 +/- 2.839 ms`, parallel-two
  `579.043 +/- 2.399 ms`, and serial repeat `578.914 +/- 3.887 ms`. Parallel-two is `0.664 ms`
  (`0.115%`) faster than the midpoint of the two serial legs, which is below run variance and is
  not a measurable iteration-time improvement.
- All-rank profiles show that the bound works as designed. On shared follower rank 12, the second
  wave is submitted about `0.9 ms` after the first instead of about `99 ms`; participant kernel
  start spread for `[4,12]` falls from `6.392` to `1.338 ms`. The third wave remains around `98 ms`
  behind the first because window two intentionally admits only the first two waves; its start
  spread falls from `13.940` to `7.113 ms` through earlier downstream progress.
- Parallel submission introduces real collective contention: average rank-12 summed Gather kernel
  residency rises from `7.428` to `10.136 ms` (`+36.5%`) while `3.202 ms` runs concurrently. The
  union still decreases from `7.428` to `6.934 ms` (`-0.494 ms`). Gather overlap with native
  model-EP changes only from `2.466` to `2.548 ms`, and full-replica model-EP matched service is
  unchanged (`47.480` versus `47.397 ms`), so there is no evidence that this window penalizes
  Megatron model-EP A2As. The bounded experiment fixes launch skew, but bandwidth sharing and the
  still-serialized third wave leave too little critical-path gain to improve iteration time.

## 2026-07-30 - Backward-complete Scatter drain

- Confirmed that owner Gather already uses `dist.all_to_all_single(..., async_op=True)` in the
  overlap path. The long owner-side Gather kernels observed in traces are device-side NCCL
  participant waits when follower ranks arrive later; only EDP for the same owner chunk depends
  on the Gather output. Independent backward compute has no data dependency on its completion.
- Confirmed that `MEGATRON_NONUNIFORM_EP_NCCL_SCATTER_CHUNKS` partitions Scatter descriptors
  after the corresponding whole owner EDP operation. It does not partition Gather or EDP into a
  per-chunk `Gather -> EDP -> Scatter` pipeline. During backward, those Scatter descriptors are
  submitted through model-EP A2A windows; the old final drain incorrectly synchronized each
  remaining descriptor before submitting the next one.
- Added an explicit backward-complete scheduler state. Final drain now consumes any outstanding
  ordered-window ticket once, queues every remaining Scatter descriptor behind the preceding
  Scatter stream dependency, and performs one final completion wait. The next backward clears
  the completed state before its first model-EP A2A burst.
- GB300 job `2538157` failed in container preflight because four unit-test doubles constructed via
  `__new__` lacked the new state attribute. State reads now default to backward-active for such
  partial objects; real DDP instances initialize the field explicitly.
- Corrected GB300 job `2538213` completed `0:0` on `theia[0235,0246,0249]`. Containerized Black,
  isort, compile, and focused unit preflight passed. The two-iteration EP8/EP4 checksum comparison
  used four Scatter chunks and matched stable versus scheduled gradients exactly for every dense
  and expert checksum field, with effectively zero cross-rank spread.

## 2026-07-27 - Four native DDP buckets regress EP32/EP28 owner parity

- Same-allocation GB300 bucket-sweep job `2507357` completed `0:0` in `15m24s` on `theia[0011-0014,0091-0094,0098,0102-0104,0200,0203-0204,0214]`; the queued GB200 duplicate `2507358` was canceled after GB300 allocated. The wrapper ran ten iterations each in fixed order: healthy-16, NEP-16, healthy-4, NEP-4. Model math, node allocation, segment, image, topology, batch semantics, and the fixed three NEP expert groups were identical; no correctness gate was repeated because the implementation did not change.
- The large setting (`DDP_NUM_BUCKETS=16`) created 4 native non-expert plus 7 native expert buckets. The small setting (`4`) created 2 plus 2. Warmup-excluded iterations 3-10: healthy-16 `638.125 +/- 1.694 ms`, NEP-16 `678.363 +/- 1.691 ms`; healthy-4 `630.200 +/- 3.678 ms`, NEP-4 `677.325 +/- 2.089 ms`.
- Therefore the small setting improves healthy by `7.925 ms` (`1.258%`) but NEP by only `1.038 ms` (`0.153%`). Owner parity falls from `94.068%` to `93.042%` (`-1.026` percentage points); the NEP owner-time gap widens from `40.237` to `47.125 ms`. This establishes the parity direction without allocation confounding. Timing-only data does not yet attribute why healthy benefits more; a same-allocation all-rank profiler pair is needed for exposed native expert-DP versus NEP-residual-tail attribution.
- Parameterized `scripts/nonuniform/run_lyris_a3b_ep32_ep28_scatter_progress_ab.sh` with `DDP_NUM_BUCKETS`, preserving its default of `16`, validating the value, and logging it in the run header. The underlying runner already accepted the setting; the wrapper had previously hard-coded `16`.
- Submitted identical GB200/GB300 candidates. GB200 job `2506439` allocated first on `lyris[0092-0095,0104-0107,0167-0170,0175-0178]`; the still-pending GB300 duplicate `2506438` was canceled. Job `2506439` completed `0:0` in `11m12s`.
- The test preserved the validated same-allocation 14-stage setup: TP2, 224 experts, top-k6, exact-uniform routing, local CUDA graphs, no optimizer, three NEP expert bucket groups, one Scatter chunk, sequence length 18368, healthy EP32/EP32 MBS4 GBS128, and NEP EP32/EP28 full/reduced MBS `4/1` GBS78. The two-layer exact checksum gate passed before timing.
- With `DDP_NUM_BUCKETS=4`, Megatron configured target bucket size `243,633,648` FP32 elements (`929.39 MiB`) and produced two actual native dense-DP gradient buckets in both legs. This is separate from the three NEP expert groups.
- Steady iterations 3-10: healthy `552.725 +/- 3.745 ms`; NEP `588.538 +/- 3.202 ms`. Owner-time parity is `93.915%`, a `6.479%` NEP slowdown and `35.812 ms` gap. The accepted 16-configured-bucket reference job `2489678` measured healthy `675.750 +/- 3.475 ms`, NEP `706.850 +/- 0.896 ms`, `95.600%` parity, and a `31.100 ms` gap. Four configured buckets therefore regress parity by `1.685` percentage points despite improving both raw iteration times on a different allocation.
- Decision: retain the default 16 configured buckets. The four-bucket result does not justify a 16-node two-bucket experiment: it combines native dense-DP work into still larger collectives, reducing readiness/overlap granularity without addressing the final NEP group-2 EDP/Scatter tail.
- Post-run code/log analysis: `DDP_NUM_BUCKETS` is a target bucket size, applied independently to the native non-expert and expert buffers at parameter boundaries. In the 16 setting, healthy and NEP source buffers build four non-expert buckets and seven native expert buckets; at the 4 setting they build two and two. NEP then removes the native expert params from their source groups and replaces them with the fixed three Approach-A expert groups, so its Gather/owner-EDP/Scatter readiness granularity remains three while healthy loses five native expert readiness points. This predicts that the 16 setting can help healthy more than NEP, reducing owner-parity at the 4 setting. The recorded `1.685`-point difference is not causal proof because jobs `2489678` and `2506439` used different 16-node allocations and only the 16 setting has an all-rank trace. A four-leg same-allocation bracket is required to quantify the effect and its exposed-communication attribution.

## 2026-07-24 - Burst-aligned Scatter ordering fixes the 14-stage deadlock

- Created and pushed rollback checkpoint `9c3df368e` (`Checkpoint NEP scatter scheduling work`) on personal-fork branch `daiyaanarfeen/nonuniform-approach-a-training-scripts` before changing the launch scheduler. This commit must remain an immutable rollback point.
- The prior 14-stage EP6/EP4 jobs `2482357`, `2482652`, and `2482932` each completed one finite iteration and then hung in iteration 2. Debug traces identified a cross-communicator cycle: the same Scatter descriptor could be launched before the next native model-EP all-to-all on one full-EP participant but after that all-to-all on another participant. NCCL's implicit ordering cannot resolve opposite host launch orders across overlapping communicators.
- Replaced rank-local Scatter admission with a burst-aligned ticket protocol in `megatron/core/distributed/nonuniform_ep.py`. Every queued owner batch receives a deterministic ordinal. At `model_ep_a2a_burst_begin`, all ranks in the widened full-EP group exchange the status and next descriptor ticket through one Gloo MIN reduction. They submit exactly one Scatter descriptor only when all participants report the same ready ticket; a mismatched ticket raises a deterministic error instead of entering NCCL with divergent ordering. Burst end only records completion and stages host phases, and the compute hook only retires completed work. The final local drain remains after native model-EP work is finished.
- Formatting job `2483357` completed under Black 26. Focused preflight job `2483367` passed Black, isort, Python compilation, and `26` scheduler tests (`86` deselected). Tests cover deterministic ordinals, burst-begin submission, retirement-only compute progress, matching tickets, and mismatch rejection.
- Four-layer exact EP6/EP4 correctness job `2483400` completed both the stable reference and burst-ticket path. Both ran two finite iterations with zero skipped/NaN iterations. All 14 dense/expert checksum fields matched exactly; expected expert weighted-sum spread was `1.471e-16`.
- Exact 14-stage GB200 liveness job `2483468` used TP2, topology `3 2`, EP6/EP4, 24 experts, top-k 6, exact-uniform routing, sequence length 18432, full/reduced MBS `4/1`, one microbatch each, true GBS14, three expert bucket groups, and four Scatter chunks. It crossed the previous failure point: iteration 1 completed in `95.472 s`, iteration 2 completed in `48.721 s`, both had finite loss and zero skipped/NaN iterations, and the job exited `0:0`. The GB300 duplicate `2483475` was canceled after this result.

### Bucket-group Scatter-window scheduler

- The EP32/EP28 profile `2487165` showed that the one-descriptor ticket pipeline was not progressing with scale: only `2/84` N=1 Scatter descriptors submitted during backward and `82` entered the final drain. The drain averaged `27.732 ms`, including `21.288 ms` GPU idle, and accounted for `96.6%` of the enlarged post-model-EP tail.
- Root cause: each model-EP boundary admitted at most one descriptor and immediately launched its next agreement. That agreement usually captured the prior descriptor as locally busy, so the following boundary consumed stale busy state even when the Scatter had already completed. More owners therefore increased the number of boundaries required rather than the amount of work admitted per boundary.
- Replaced one-descriptor tickets with deterministic bucket-group windows in `megatron/core/distributed/nonuniform_ep.py`. One asynchronous Gloo agreement now validates the descriptor count plus first/last ticket for one `(layer, expert bucket group)` window. On unanimous agreement, every owner/chunk descriptor in that window is queued in fixed order on the existing Scatter stream; queueing can cross owner-batch boundaries without bypassing stream dependencies. An explicit layer ordinal prevents one-bucket models from merging adjacent layers.
- The deterministic non-MoE CUDA-graph post-hook now finishes any ready split host phases and refreshes the aligned window pipeline after useful compute is enqueued. Model-EP burst hooks retain the one-boundary asynchronous pipeline. No rank-local elapsed-time decision controls collective count or order, and a queue/window mismatch raises before NCCL submission.
- Formatting-only job `2488216` ran Black 26 in the named NeMo 26.06 Enroot cache. Final GB200 preflight `2488233` passed Black, isort, Python compilation, and `29` selected scheduler tests (`86` deselected), including cross-owner windowing, group/layer boundaries, cross-batch stream queueing, mismatch rejection, and compute-hook progression. Earlier preflight `2488170` stopped at formatting, and `2488221` passed the prior `28`-test selection before the window test was renamed into the standard filter.
- EP8/EP4 distributed checksum job `2488242` completed `0:0` on GB200 nodes `lyris[0137-0139]`. It used two layers, TP2, topology `4 2`, two Gather chunks, one Scatter chunk, two training iterations, and 12 ranks. Stable and window-scheduler paths both had finite losses with zero skips/NaNs; all 14 aggregate, dense, and expert checksum fields matched with zero relative delta. Debug logs show each group window submitting all four owners and both Gather chunks in deterministic order.
- Created and pushed checkpoint `9f7d78678` (`Advance NEP scatter by bucket windows`) to the `daiyaanarfeen/Megatron-LM` personal fork after the EP8/EP4 gate. The commit contains only the scheduler, focused tests, and journal; unrelated local analysis edits remain uncommitted.
- First profile attempt `2488293` failed before training because the direct wrapper invocation omitted exact-uniform routing's required `--moe-router-bias-update-rate 0.0`; it provided no scheduler data. Corrected all-rank GB200 profile `2488328` completed both healthy and NEP legs `0:0` on `lyris[0010-0012,0014]`, with every iteration finite and zero skips/NaNs.
- In each profiled EP8/EP4 owner step, all `12/12` N=1 descriptors submitted inside the measured backward interval as three deterministic `n4` windows. Rank-0 window starts were approximately `176`, `213`, and `265 ms` relative to each step; backward ended at `278.231/276.880 ms`. No descriptor was submitted by the final drain. The drain only synchronized the already queued tail for `0.195/0.030 ms` of CPU wait.
- Rank-0 Scatter residency averaged `7.607 ms`; `4.662 ms` (`61.29%`) overlapped useful non-dispatch GPU work and `2.944 ms` remained exposed. Profiled owner full-step spans were `273.231 ms` healthy versus `299.055 ms` NEP, while backward was `137.387 -> 150.086 ms`. These profiled timings diagnose causality only; this run intentionally prioritized all-rank traces over an unprofiled acceptance measurement.
- Analysis artifacts are `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_ep4_bucket_window_profile2_b200/slowdown_analysis_2488328.json` and `scatter_step_overlap_2488328.json`.
- Intermediate GB200 job `2488435` completed the 28-rank EP16/EP12 checksum gate and 10-iteration healthy/NEP timing legs `0:0` on `lyris[0073-0076,0247-0249,0251]`. All 14 aggregate, dense, and expert checksum fields matched with zero relative delta, and all iterations were finite with zero skips/NaNs. The queued GB300 duplicate `2488440` was canceled after GB200 allocated.
- Timing iterations 3-10 measured `551.550 +/- 4.070 ms` healthy versus `584.963 +/- 3.565 ms` NEP. Mean owner parity is `94.288%`, a `33.413 ms` gap and `6.058%` slowdown; median parity is `94.169%` (`550.7/584.8 ms`). This misses the `95%` gate and is approximately `5.1 ms` slower on the NEP side than the prior one-descriptor N=1 result, while the healthy means are within `1 ms`.
- Follow-up all-rank attribution showed that finishing participant-dependent host phases from non-MoE compute hooks was unsafe and expensive. Those hooks blocked the autograd host for `17-65 ms` in native `start_grad_sync` and another `20-51 ms` on fresh Gloo agreement, delaying later backward kernel submission. A rank-local `Work.is_completed()` retry in job `2488882` deadlocked because ranks launched different numbers of Gloo agreements; rank 0 drained a waiting ticket while reduced ranks waited to initialize EDP NCCL. This was a real collective-participant/order bug, not a timeout.
- Retained scheduler rule: non-MoE compute hooks only retire completed Scatter events. They never finish participant-dependent host phases, launch EDP, submit a window, or start a Gloo collective. Model-EP burst ends are the sole deterministic launch points: consume one prior unanimous window agreement, submit that complete bucket-group window in fixed order, then launch the next asynchronous agreement. Focused GB200/GB300 preflights `2489028` and `2489032` passed Black, isort, compilation, and the selected scheduler tests.
- EP8/EP4 exact-checksum job `2489062` matched all 14 aggregate, dense, and expert fields exactly. All-rank profile `2489099` submitted two `n4` windows (`8/12` descriptors) during backward and left only the final four-descriptor dependency tail for drain. Rank-0 Scatter useful-compute overlap was `3.242/6.422 ms` (`50.5%`), with zero Scatter/model-EP overlap; profiled owner parity was `94.49%`.
- EP16/EP12 same-allocation timing job `2489223` measured iterations 3-10 at `548.250 +/- 2.975 ms` healthy and `573.550 +/- 2.826 ms` NEP. Mean owner parity was `95.59%`, clearing the acceptance gate.
- Tested and rejected same-boundary ticket priming rather than retaining an unproven optimization. Preflight `2489445` passed and exact-checksum job `2489462` matched all 14 fields, but all-rank profile `2489541` still left group 2 for drain because its Gather/EDP dependency became ready only at the backward tail. Priming moved earlier Scatter into NCCL-heavy intervals: useful Scatter overlap fell to `2.1%`, profiled owner parity fell to `92.48%`, and backward grew by `11.521 ms`. The priming code and test were fully reverted.
- Scaled retained scheduler job `2489678` to the existing EP32/EP28 workload: TP2, 224 experts, top-k 6, sequence length 18368, full/reduced MBS `4/1`, one microbatch each, three expert bucket groups, N=1 Scatter, exact-uniform routing, local CUDA graphs, and no optimizer. The 60-rank two-layer gate completed both paths with all 14 checksum fields exact. Unprofiled iterations 3-10 were `675.750 +/- 3.475 ms` healthy and `706.850 +/- 0.896 ms` NEP: `95.60%` owner parity and a `31.100 ms` gap. The GB300 duplicate `2489679` was canceled after GB200 allocated.
- All-rank GB200 profile `2489902` completed `0:0` with all `64` healthy and `60` NEP traces. Every full EP32 rank consumed exactly `g0_n28` and `g1_n28` windows per measured step; all 60 ranks submitted exactly 28 descriptors for each of groups 0, 1, and 2 in identical order. Reduced EP28 ranks correctly skip widened agreement because their runtime has `needs_reshard=False`. There were no role-correct count/order mismatches.
- The scheduler now submits `56/84` full-rank descriptors in two backward-time windows instead of the prior `2/84`. Only group 2's `28` descriptors remain in the final dependency tail. Rank 0's group-2 tail averages `12.697 ms` (`10.548 ms` completion waits), down from the prior `27.732 ms` final drain. The `15.035 ms` drain reduction closely matches the unprofiled owner-gap reduction from `46.312` to `31.100 ms`.
- Across all 28 full-replica owners, Scatter residency averages `3.482 ms`; `2.886 ms` is overlapped with useful non-NCCL compute (`84.37%` mean, `77.87-93.13%` range), only `0.596 ms` is exposed, and measured Scatter/model-EP overlap is exactly `0 ms` on every owner. The retained scheduler therefore fixes scale-dependent descriptor admission without compromising checksum correctness or stalling native model-EP work. Reproducible artifacts are under `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep32_ep28_boundary_window_profile_b200/`, including `scatter_window_admission_2489902.json` and `scatter_step_overlap_full_owners_2489902.json`.

## 2026-07-23 - Compute-window Scatter progression and compute-heavy EP8/EP4 result

- Parameterized the 14-stage `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E` workload for sequence length and per-replica microbatch size. The comparison uses sequence length `16384`, TP2, 128 experts, top-k 6, exact-uniform routing, local CUDA graphs, no optimizer, disabled energy/diagnostic logging, and all-rank profiles. Healthy is EP8/EP8 on 16 GPUs; NEP is EP8/EP4 on 12 GPUs with weighted GBS derived from the full and reduced replica contributions.
- The initial post-compute scheduler submitted only one Scatter descriptor after each non-MoE graph replay. Job `2474061` passed the two-layer checksum gate, then measured `260.088 ms` healthy versus `281.656 ms` NEP (`92.34%` owner parity). Its N=4 owner traces showed only `0.955 ms` useful compute overlap across `6.674 ms` Scatter residency (`14.31%`), leaving `5.719 ms` exposed. Only seven completion waits were separated by useful work; most of the 48 descriptors still drained near backward's end.
- Reworked `_progress_nep_scatter_after_compute_launch()` into a same-thread bounded progress loop. A completion event is recorded after the useful non-MoE CUDA graph is enqueued. The autograd thread retires and submits successive small Scatter chunks only while that compute event remains pending, then returns before it can announce the next model-EP A2A. No background Python thread launches NCCL, preserving the fix for the prior concurrent-host launch fatal.
- Focused GB200 job `2475084` passed `17` scheduler tests and the two-layer EP8/EP4 numerical gate. Stable and split paths matched all 14 aggregate, dense, and expert gradient fields on every rank and completed finite iterations.
- Same-allocation N=4 job `2475103` completed `0:0`. Steady-state iterations 3-18 measured `258.238 ms` healthy and `275.600 ms` NEP (`93.70%` mean owner parity; medians give `93.32%`). The bounded loop moved the first quartile of Scatter submissions from roughly `252 ms` to `184-204 ms` within the profiled step. All-owner useful Scatter overlap rose to `1.614/6.597 ms` (`24.47%`) and exposed Scatter fell to `4.983 ms`.
- Smaller-chunk job `2475255` tested N=8 because N=4 still had substantial exposed Scatter. It regressed to `256.812 ms` healthy versus `280.106 ms` NEP (`91.68%` parity). N=8 doubled Scatter kernels from 12 to 24 per owner step, increased Scatter residency from `6.597` to `9.998 ms`, and increased exposed Scatter from `4.983` to `7.893 ms`; N=4 remains the selected chunk count.
- Final below-proportional job `2475363` used full/healthy MBS4 and reduced MBS1. The reduced replica therefore receives one-quarter of the full replica's samples per TP/CP lane, below the one-half EP4/EP8 proportional share. Steady-state iterations 3-18 measured `473.212 ms` healthy and `491.419 ms` NEP: `96.30%` mean owner parity, `3.85%` slowdown, and an `18.206 ms` gap. Medians give `96.35%` parity. Standard deviations were `1.911/2.656 ms`.
- Participant-aligned owner EDP confirms the reduced replica is no longer the bottleneck: full owner rank 0 is latest for five of six profiled EDP operations; reduced owner rank 8 is latest once and adds only `0.0585 ms` total owner participant wait.
- In the final profile, all-owner Scatter residency was `6.943 ms`, useful compute overlap was `2.183 ms` (`31.44%`), and exposed Scatter was `4.760 ms`. The profiler's canonical owner backward span was `244.710 -> 257.437 ms` (`+12.727 ms`). NEP reshard-union exposure remained `19.365 ms`, including canonical-owner Gather `1.810 ms`, EDP `11.453 ms`, and Scatter `6.102 ms`; the improved parity comes from more useful work amortizing and partially hiding fixed NEP costs, not from fully hiding the reshard.
- Reproducible artifacts:
  - `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_ep4_scatter_progress_n4_compute_window/slowdown_analysis_2475103.json`
  - `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_ep4_scatter_progress_n4_compute_window/scatter_step_overlap_owners_2475103.json`

## 2026-07-23 - Reduced-EP grouped-backward host-time decomposition

Per profiler step, `_GroupedLinearBackward` host scope time rises `12.426 -> 20.940 ms` (`+8.514 ms`), while summed expert GEMM device residency rises only `12.969 -> 15.286 ms` (`+2.317 ms`).

Launch API time rises `1.987 -> 4.400 ms` (`+2.413 ms`); all measured CUDA API time inside the scopes rises by about `2.696 ms`. No long stream/device synchronization appears inside the scopes. The remaining host-scope delta is TE/autograd preparation, bookkeeping, and inter-launch CPU spacing that the current annotations do not subdivide further. Exact-uniform routing equalizes split counts but does not reduce the number of local experts, descriptors, or launches.

## 2026-07-23 - Same-allocation healthy/N=2 slowdown attribution

- The Scatter sweep had same-allocation timing controls but no healthy trace. Submitted minimal controlled job `2472815`: healthy EP8/EP8 followed by NEP EP8/EP4 Scatter N=2 on GB200 nodes `lyris[0164-0167]`, segment 4. Both used the exact-uniform 14-stage workload, TP2, MBS1, one microbatch/replica, 128 experts, top-k 6, local graphs, no optimizer, 16 DDP buckets, frozen router bias, and disabled diagnostic/energy logging. The job completed `0:0` in `6m45s`; all 28 rank traces contain three matched profiler steps.
- The apparent absolute regression is allocation drift, not a larger NEP penalty. Prior job `2463417` measured `100.740/130.238 ms` healthy/NEP, a `29.498 ms` gap. Sweep `2466541` measured `104.915/133.965 ms` healthy/N=2, a `29.050 ms` gap. Healthy moved `+4.175 ms` while NEP moved `+3.727 ms`; normalized overhead improved by `0.448 ms`. N=2 is also `0.110 ms` faster than the repeated N=1 mean, which is noise-level.
- All-rank profiling inflates the absolute effect, so trace milliseconds are causal diagnostics rather than production timing. Owner rank 0 measures `124.479 -> 179.516 ms` (`+55.037`) full-step and `59.021 -> 106.498 ms` (`+47.477`) backward; backward accounts for `86.3%` of the profiled gap. Exact backward interval accounting is `+42.687 ms` NCCL union, `+9.582 ms` non-NCCL union, `-11.347 ms` from additional NCCL/non-NCCL overlap, and `+6.556 ms` idle. The terms reconstruct `47.477 ms` exactly.
- Mutually exclusive backward NCCL-union deltas are: new Gather-only `+6.021 ms`, new Scatter-only `+6.092 ms`, dense-DP-only `+14.255 ms`, model-EP-only `+2.772 ms`, multi-group NCCL co-residency `+13.557 ms`, other/unmapped `+0.356 ms`, and expert-EDP-only `-0.365 ms`; total `+42.687 ms`. EDP-only falls because most enlarged EDP service is co-resident with native NCCL, not because EDP is cheaper.
- Direct reshard exposure is `25.837 ms`: Gather `3.268`, EDP `16.990`, Scatter `5.580`. Healthy EDP already exposes `6.388 ms`, so incremental exposed reshard is `19.449 ms`: `+10.601` EDP plus `3.268` Gather plus `5.580` Scatter. NEP owner-layout staging adds `6.481 ms` GPU service (copies/adds/fills/norms); `3.442 ms` is uniquely exposed after intersecting it with all other GPU activity.
- EDP payload doubles from `957,874,176` to `1,915,748,352` elements after gradients are gathered onto the owner layout. Participant-aligned matched EDP service rises `7.405 -> 17.100 ms` (`+9.695`), while owner wait rises only `0.030 -> 1.775 ms`; the added EDP is real transfer service. It co-resides with model-EP for `7.171 ms` and dense-DP for `5.601 ms`, explaining much of the multi-group interval and residual native-service inflation.
- Native dense-DP has unchanged six operations and `553,916,355` elements, but owner residency rises `8.103 -> 30.020 ms`. Participant wait rises `1.545 -> 21.083 ms` (`+19.538`); matched service rises only `6.618 -> 9.041 ms` (`+2.423`). Reduced rank 10 is latest for 12/18 profiled dense operations and accounts for `17.295 ms/step` of owner wait. Its EP4 grouped-backward host work is `20.940 ms` versus `12.300 ms` on full rank 2 and launches 1,152 versus 576 expert GEMMs; it also participates in enlarged EDP. Thus the unchanged owner dense all-reduces wait for the later reduced-replica participant.
- Native full-replica model-EP has unchanged 36 operations/payload. Backward participant wait rises by `8.735 ms` and matched service by `1.215 ms`; exposure rises `8.846 -> 18.294 ms`. Completion is healthy through backward A2A ordinal 5, then lags `14.999 ms` after the first grouped reshard and `26.663 ms` after the second, finishing `24.847 ms` late. This is reshard-induced owner/follower readiness skew plus EDP/model-EP co-residency, not Scatter transport contention.
- Scatter/model-EP contention is mostly, not literally, eliminated: per-step overlap is `0.257/0.923/0/0 ms` on full owners 0-3. It is no longer the dominant cost. Four of six Scatter kernels, one of three Gathers, one of three EDP operations, and four of six dense-DP operations remain after rank 0's final model-EP A2A. The final owner tail is `29.877 ms` versus healthy `7.339 ms` (`+22.538`): `+18.888` NCCL union, `+3.672` non-NCCL union, `+2.781` idle, offset by `+2.803` additional overlap.
- The code explains the Scatter tail. `_submit_nep_scatter_chunk()` submits at most one chunk and returns if the prior completion event is not ready. It is called at `model_ep_a2a_burst_end()` and during the final drain, with no completion callback or intermediate poll. A chunk that completes after a burst-end check cannot trigger the next chunk until another A2A ends; remaining chunks are force-synchronized serially by `_drain_nep_scatter_scheduler()` at final gradient sync. This fixes the cross-thread NCCL launch race and avoids most A2A contention, but converts missed gap opportunities into exposed tail.
- Reproducible artifacts: `slurm_runs/nep_scatter_samethread_healthy_n2_profile/slowdown_analysis_2472815.json`, `scatter_step_overlap_owners_2472815.json`, job output `slurm_runs/lyris/coreai_comparch_sysarch-nep.scatter-attribution-gb200-2472815.out`, and the healthy/NEP rank traces under the job root. Extended `scripts/nonuniform/analyze_exact_uniform_nep_slowdown.py` to accept explicit trace directories/step counts and classify the separate Gather group.

## 2026-07-22 - Same-thread completion-gated Scatter sweep completed

- Sweep job `2466084` exposed a host-launch correctness bug at Scatter `N=2`: a background NEP worker and native Megatron launched NCCL concurrently on the same device, producing `Fatal: host threads racing to launch NCCL on same device`. Replaced the A2A/Scatter worker executors with a same/autograd-thread state machine. It submits at most one Scatter descriptor, records its completion event, and submits the next descriptor only after completion. `model_ep_a2a_burst_end` records the native A2A completion event and device-orders the next eligible Scatter chunk after it; no background Python thread launches NCCL.
- Updated focused scheduler tests and the sweep preflight for same-thread device ordering, predecessor-completion rechecks, and stale-worker exclusion. The container preflight passed `16` tests (`89` deselected). GB200 correctness job `2466480` completed `0:0`; its EP8/EP4 `N=2` stable-versus-scheduler comparison matched all 14 aggregate, dense, and expert gradient checksum fields exactly on every rank and completed with finite iterations.
- Full GB200 sweep job `2466541` completed all nine timing/profile legs `0:0` in `22m49s`; all 36 rank traces were written and no concurrent-host NCCL fatal, traceback, NaN, or skipped iteration occurred. The exact 14-stage `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E` workload used TP2, 128 experts, top-k 6, exact-uniform routing, local CUDA graphs, no optimizer, and 16 MiB DDP buckets. Healthy EP8/EP8 used topology `4 4`, 16 GPUs, GBS8; proportional NEP EP8/EP4 used topology `4 2`, 12 GPUs, GBS6. MBS was 1 with one microbatch per replica.
- Clean iterations 6-25 measured the bracketed healthy mean at `104.915 ms` and `293.738 TFLOP/s/GPU`. Scatter `N=1` repeated mean was `134.075 ms`, `229.840 TFLOP/s/GPU`, and `78.25%` owner-time parity; `N=2` was `133.965 ms`, `230.030 TFLOP/s/GPU`, and `78.32%`; `N=4` was `136.015 ms`, `226.545 TFLOP/s/GPU`, and `77.13%`. Iterations 13-25 preserve the ranking. The `0.11 ms` N=2 advantage over the repeated N=1 mean is noise-level, while N=4 is a regression.
- Added `scripts/nonuniform/analyze_nep_scatter_step_overlap.py` because deferred submission invalidates the old one-Scatter-per-Gather-gap assumption. On canonical owner rank 0, N=1/N=2/N=4 respectively expose `4.244/4.641/5.721 ms` of `5.649/5.648/6.060 ms` Scatter residency and overlap `24.99/17.83/5.55%` with useful non-NCCL compute. N=2 reduces Scatter/model-EP overlap to `0%` on rank 0 but does not place Scatter under useful compute; N=4 fragments too far and reintroduces model-EP overlap. Results are in `slurm_runs/nep_scatter_samethread_sweep_gb200/scatter_step_overlap_rank0.json` and `scatter_step_overlap_all_ranks.json`.
- Conclusion: the same-thread scheduler fixes the NCCL launch race and preserves gradients, but N=2/4 chunking does not recover performance parity. N=2 is statistically equivalent to N=1, and N=4 is slower because extra fragmentation increases exposed Scatter and native model-EP residency. Run root: `slurm_runs/nep_scatter_samethread_sweep_gb200`; wrapper output: `slurm_runs/lyris/coreai_comparch_sysarch-nep.scatter-samethread-sweep-gb200-2466541.out`.

## 2026-07-22 - Scatter-only chunking implementation prepared

- Added independent `MEGATRON_NONUNIFORM_EP_NCCL_SCATTER_CHUNKS` control. Gather and native Megatron EDP remain one unchanged operation; only the existing owner-to-follower all-to-all Scatter call is decomposed. Every chunk count, including `N=1`, uses the same loop and launch function. Each in-flight subchunk has a distinct buffer-cache key.
- Chunk boundaries balance actual remote payload rather than raw owner-layout offsets. This matters for EP8/EP4 because the owner-local half of the contiguous layout carries no network payload: naïve midpoint splitting would create one zero-byte chunk and one full-size transfer. The implementation preserves contiguous full-range coverage for owner copy-back while giving each network chunk positive, approximately equal remote payload.
- Added focused tests for `N=1/2/4` range construction, shared ordered code-path behavior, and invalid controls. Python compilation, shell parsing, and `git diff --check` pass. Updated the all-rank Scatter-gap analyzer to classify Gather/Scatter by participant direction and aggregate multiple Scatter kernels per phase. Regression against job `2443206` reproduces the prior `N=1` result and reports exactly one Scatter kernel per phase.
- Prepared a two-layer EP8/EP4 gradient-checksum comparison of Scatter `N=1` versus `N=4`, followed by a same-allocation exact-uniform 14-stage sweep with bracketed healthy/`N=1` timing controls and `N=1/2/4` all-rank profiles. GB200 and GB300 scheduler estimates will be checked before submission.

## 2026-07-22 - Deferred-Scatter joint gap capacity

- Added `scripts/nonuniform/analyze_nep_scatter_gap_capacity.py` and analyzed the all-rank 14-stage deferred-fence trace from job `2443206`: `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_ep4_reshard_cause_ab/deferred_no_check/a3b_repeat14_ep8_ep4_mbs1_mb1_1_proportional/2443206/torch_profile`. The reproducible result is `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_ep4_reshard_cause_ab/scatter_gap_capacity_2443206.json`.
- The analysis uses the first two Scatter phases, where later model-EP work exists. For each `[owner, follower]` transfer pair it intersects both ranks' clocks from the later Scatter-kernel start to the earlier next-Gather start. Across eight pair/phase windows, both participants have no native model-EP NCCL kernel resident for `40.73%` on average (range `24.63-57.12%`) and neither participant has any NCCL kernel resident for `31.74%` on average (range `21.95-51.43%`).
- Strict jointly no-NCCL capacity is `4.329-13.933 ms` per window, versus `1.978-2.064 ms` owner-side Scatter residency, or `2.19-6.94x` the current transfer time. Every window has three to five strict gaps at least `0.5 ms`; every window has one to three gaps at least `1.0 ms`. The largest strict gap is `1.008-5.659 ms`. Thus enough aggregate capacity exists, but the smallest windows cannot reliably accommodate one monolithic approximately `2 ms` Scatter without colliding with EP.
- This run already used `HIGH_PRIORITY_STREAM_GROUPS=ep` and `CUDA_DEVICE_MAX_CONNECTIONS=32`; native EP and owner-transfer execute on distinct streams (rank 0 streams 7 and 52), while `nep_owner_transfer` remains default priority. Priority alone did not protect EP because each Scatter kernel was already resident when the next owner-rank EP A2A arrived, by only `0.0019-0.0045 ms`.
- The transfer pairs also expose a scheduling caveat. Followers launch Scatter `4.268-8.305 ms` before owners and remain resident for `6.306-10.380 ms`, while owner kernels are approximately `2.0 ms`; paired kernels finish together. This residency includes participant waiting, not continuous link transfer. Scatter uses a 32-block, 640-thread NCCL SendRecv kernel, so the first low-priority chunk remains non-preemptible and can occupy SMs while waiting even if later chunks yield at finer boundaries.
- Conclusion: the trace supports a Scatter-only chunking experiment at `N=2` and `N=4`, preserving the existing Gather and native EDP paths and identical collective order on both participants. This is distinct from the earlier whole-reshard chunking attempt. The A/B must verify gradient correctness, unchanged native model-EP matched service, reduced Scatter/model-EP overlap, and unchanged total Scatter service. PyTorch traces contain no NVLink throughput counters, so these are NCCL-residency bounds rather than a claim of measured physical-link utilization.


## 2026-07-22 - Exact-uniform NEP slowdown attribution

- Added the memory-bounded all-rank analyzer `scripts/nonuniform/analyze_exact_uniform_nep_slowdown.py` and analyzed all 16 profiled steps on all 16 healthy and 12 NEP ranks from job `2463417`. The reproducible output is `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_exact_uniform_nep_ab/slowdown_analysis_2463417.json`. The profiler-free result remains `100.740 ms` healthy versus `130.238 ms` NEP (`+29.498 ms`, `+29.28%`, `77.35%` owner parity), while launch-centered standard deviation remains lower under NEP (`0.430` versus `0.628 ms`).
- In the diagnostic traces, the full-step gap is `36.590 ms` and the backward gap is `36.091 ms`, so backward accounts for `98.6%` of the slowdown. Exact backward interval accounting is `+34.115 ms` NCCL union, `+8.341 ms` non-NCCL union, `-7.162 ms` from additional NCCL/non-NCCL overlap, and `+0.797 ms` idle, reconstructing the `36.091 ms` gap to floating-point precision. The profiler changes absolute timing, so these traces are used for causal attribution rather than the production effect size.
- Owner rank 0's three Gather/EDP/Scatter pipelines have `29.375 ms` total residency but only `3.733 ms` useful-compute overlap (`12.70%`), leaving `25.642 ms` exposed. Gather is `6.029 ms` resident and `2.308 ms` exposed; owner EDP is `17.773 ms` resident and `17.763 ms` exposed; Scatter is `5.572 ms` resident and fully exposed. Healthy native expert EDP already has `7.163 ms` exposure, so the incremental exposed reshard versus healthy is `18.480 ms` (`10.600 ms` extra EDP exposure plus `7.880 ms` new Gather/Scatter exposure).
- Participant alignment confirms that this is real communication service rather than a routing or rendezvous artifact. NEP owner EDP carries exactly twice the healthy owner payload (`1,915,748,352` versus `957,874,176` elements) and matched service rises `7.736 -> 15.295 ms`. The six owner-transfer all-to-alls add `11.601 ms` matched service with only `0.206 ms` accumulated owner participant wait. Native model-EP matched service improves slightly (`5.709 -> 5.453 ms`) and TP is unchanged/slightly faster (`0.922 -> 0.846 ms`), excluding those transports as the regression source.
- The owner-layout staging path adds `801` dispatch-stream GPU operations and `9.680 ms` residency per backward: `576` DtoD copies (`4.741 ms`), `192` accumulation adds (`2.428 ms`), six fills (`0.998 ms`), six gradient norms (`1.448 ms`), and small residual work. Their overlap with existing kernels limits the exact non-NCCL union increase to `8.341 ms`, but this is still deterministic NEP-only work on the critical path.
- The implementation explains why the reshard remains exposed. This run enables split host phases but disables post-graph progression and model-EP fence deferral. Gather is launched from the MoE backward boundary on the dispatch stream, then owner EDP and Scatter are queued behind it. At the next MoE backward pre-hook, `_wait_for_nep_dispatch_launch()` orders the compute stream behind the whole completion event. Gather therefore overlaps only `3.722 ms` of remaining work; owner EDP and Scatter cannot extend into subsequent MoE backward compute. Participant-aligned model-EP milestones acquire their lag exactly at the first two reshard boundaries (`-2.919 -> +18.477 ms` and `+12.706 -> +28.890 ms`), followed by another approximately `15.9 ms` final reshard tail after the last model-EP operation.
- Dense-DP inflation is a propagated secondary effect, not a separate root cost to add to the reshard totals. Its payload and operation count are unchanged, but owner participant wait rises `1.929 -> 16.733 ms` and matched service rises `6.547 -> 8.580 ms`; the latter coincides with `3.353 ms` of NEP owner-EDP/dense-DP overlap and SM contention. Reduced ranks 8 or 10 are latest for `70/96` dense operations. Exact-uniform EP4 still launches `1,152` expert GEMMs per step versus `576` on EP8; reduced grouped-backward host scopes take `22.301` versus `12.790 ms`, while summed GEMM residency changes only `14.667` versus `12.268 ms`. This deterministic launch/dependency overhead creates cross-rank phase skew and moves dense-DP waiting earlier, but the reduced ranks finish backward before full owner rank 0 and do not replace the exposed owner reshard as the main bottleneck.
- Conclusion: exact routing, same-allocation ABBA timing, disabled energy/diagnostic logging, and all-rank participant alignment exclude routing imbalance, iteration variance, native model-EP transport, and TP transport as primary explanations. The dominant correct optimization target is the implementation-imposed reshard completion fence, followed by owner-layout staging overhead; any follow-up must preserve collective ordering, gradient correctness, and native Megatron NCCL service.

- Same-allocation production job `2455049` completed `0:0` in `17m22s` on GB200 nodes `lyris[0132-0135]`, segment 4. It ran random/exact/exact/random 100-iteration timing legs followed by 16-rank random and exact profiles over steps 30-45. All six cases completed, all iterations were finite with zero skips/NaNs, and all 32 expected traces were written. Model and runtime settings were fixed at TP2/EP8/ETP1, MBS1/GBS8, 128 experts, top-k 6, pattern `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E`, local graphs, no optimizer step, frozen expert-bias update, and disabled energy/diagnostic logging.
- Exactness is directly verified in the real traces: all `18,432/18,432` model-EP input/output split vectors across 9,216 collective records and 16 ranks were exactly `[3072] * 8`, with one unique vector, zero CV, and zero maximum deviation. The random-balanced control had no exact vectors, 1,536 unique vectors, `1.5537%` mean within-vector CV, `3.0815%` maximum CV, and a maximum peer deviation of 176 assignments. Exact runs reported `seq_load_balancing_loss=1.000000` on every logged iteration.
- Exact routing reduced within-launch timing standard deviation from `1.2954` to `0.3333 ms` (`74.27%` lower standard deviation, `93.38%` lower variance). Same-mode iteration-position correlation fell from `r=0.718` random to `r=0.344` exact, supporting the conclusion that the deterministic random-logit split sequence contributed repeatable timing variation. The two random timing legs were `106.482 +/- 1.201` and `104.869 +/- 1.383 ms`; exact legs were `103.482 +/- 0.389` and `107.790 +/- 0.266 ms`.
- The ABBA pooled means were effectively identical: `105.675 ms` random and `105.636 ms` exact (`-0.039 ms`, `-0.037%`). The exact process-launch means differed by `4.308 ms`, so pooled standard deviation and any single-profile mean are confounded by launch-level system drift; this experiment supports throughput parity, not a mean-speedup claim. The deterministic generator itself adds only about `0.016 ms/step` of net GPU kernel time relative to random-logit generation on rank 0.
- Participant-aligned traces show the expected mechanism. Mean model-EP device-start spread fell `0.558 -> 0.337 ms` (`39.6%`), host-submit spread fell `0.922 -> 0.564 ms`, forward-combine start spread fell `0.796 -> 0.529 ms`, and backward-metadata start spread fell `1.754 -> 0.910 ms`. Matched transport service was unchanged at `0.161/0.160 ms`. Profiled critical-step standard deviation fell `3.495 -> 2.061 ms`; remaining exact-mode timing variation still correlates with model-EP readiness (`r=0.708`) and grouped-launch gaps (`r=0.651`), so exact routing removes split geometry as a confound but does not eliminate host/runtime/NCCL scheduling variation.
- Reproducible artifacts: `scripts/nonuniform/run_lyris_uniform_router_unit.sh`, `scripts/nonuniform/run_lyris_a3b_ep8_exact_uniform_ab.sh`, `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_exact_uniform/analysis_2455049.json`, and the per-condition profile JSON files in the same result root.

## 2026-07-21 - Stationary EP8 routing variance and model-EP skew root cause

- Corrected stationary A/B job `2452182` completed `0:0` in `24m10s` on four GB300 nodes `theia[0194-0197]`. All four profiler-free ABBA cases and four all-rank profiler cases reached their configured terminal iteration; all 64 expected traces were written. Every measured iteration was finite with zero skips/NaNs. The workload held TP2/EP8/EP8, MBS1/GBS8, 128 experts, top-k 6, the 14-stage pattern, local graphs, no optimizer step, and all logging controls fixed. Energy logging was disabled and the expert-bias update rate was fixed at zero, eliminating the two confounders found in earlier jobs.
- Over stationary iterations 30-100, the balanced repeats measured `105.586 +/- 1.336 ms` (`1.27%` CV) and `102.634 +/- 1.337 ms` (`1.30%` CV). The natural fixed-bias repeats measured `133.090 +/- 3.137 ms` (`2.36%` CV) and `132.230 +/- 2.905 ms` (`2.20%` CV). Pooled balanced/natural means were `104.110/132.660 ms`, so natural routing was `27.42%` slower and reached `78.48%` throughput parity. The ABBA balanced-run drift was `2.95 ms`, far below the `28.55 ms` controlled effect.
- The routed-token CV was `0.569%` balanced and `14.228%` natural. Mean model-EP device-start spread increased from `0.647` to `0.951 ms` per all-to-all, while matched service after the last participant arrived changed only `0.162 -> 0.172 ms`. Critical-step time correlates strongly with model-EP start spread (`r=0.958/0.827` balanced/natural) but only weakly with instantaneous routing CV in the stationary natural run (`r=0.377`). The immediate mechanism is participant readiness; total-load CV is not a sufficient per-step predictor of the detailed split-shape schedule.
- The exact readiness source is grouped-linear host submission. Natural routing raises forward-combine start spread `1.149 -> 1.844 ms`; forward `_GroupedLinear` CPU-duration spread rises `0.272 -> 0.908 ms`, explaining about `0.636/0.695 ms` of the added phase skew. Backward-metadata start spread rises `1.829 -> 2.945 ms`; `_GroupedLinearBackward` CPU-duration spread rises `0.396 -> 1.399 ms`, explaining about `1.003/1.116 ms`. Forward/backward internal launch-gap spread also rises `0.136 -> 0.268 ms` and `0.078 -> 0.485 ms`. All-to-all trace records directly show the unequal per-rank split vectors in the natural case. The supported causal chain is unequal expert splits -> variable grouped-linear host/launch progress -> later model-EP submission on some ranks -> early NCCL kernels waiting for participants.
- The profiled critical-step gap is `20.238 ms`: GPU idle increases by `13.197 ms`, NCCL union by `6.268 ms`, non-NCCL union by only `0.254 ms`, and NCCL/non-NCCL overlap falls by `0.519 ms`. Critical-rank model-EP residency supplies the largest NCCL increase (`22.566 -> 26.997 ms`). This is waiting residency, not slower transport or extra GEMM work.
- Participant-aligned native Megatron DDP analysis rules out a hidden all-reduce transport regression. Expert-DP matched service is `0.682/0.680 ms` per operation balanced/natural and dense-DP service is `1.584/1.545 ms`; max-group service per step is likewise stable. Raw DDP residency varies with which early rank becomes critical, but service after all participants arrive does not increase. The balanced-routing RNG kernel costs only about `0.033 ms/step`, so it is negligible and biases against, rather than in favor of, the faster balanced result.
- Extended `scripts/nonuniform/analyze_ep8_routing_variance.py` to align expert/dense native DDP collectives by participants, separate readiness from matched service, decompose the critical rank by NCCL process group, and measure both forward and backward grouped-linear scopes. Reproducible result: `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_routing_variance/analysis_2452182.json`.

## 2026-07-21 - Healthy EP8 baseline variance result

- Job `2450978` completed `0:0` in `14m03s` on GB200 nodes `lyris[0229-0232]`. It ran three profiler-free 50-iteration healthy EP8/EP8 launches, a 16-step low-overhead all-rank profile, and a two-step all-rank profile with Python stacks and shapes. All iterations were finite with zero skips/NaNs; both profile phases produced all 16 rank traces. Configuration was TP2, EP8, ETP1, MBS1, one microbatch per replica, GBS8, 128 experts, top-k 6, forced-balanced routing, 16 DDP buckets, local graphs, no optimizer, and pattern `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E`.
- Predeclared iterations 5-50 produced launch means `142.389/143.313/140.128 ms`, within-launch standard deviations `7.351/6.792/7.615 ms`, and CVs `5.16/4.74/5.43%`. Pooled `n=138` is `141.943 ms` mean, `141.500 ms` median, `7.332 ms` standard deviation, `5.17%` CV, and `131.125/154.575 ms` p05/p95. Between-launch mean standard deviation is `1.638 ms` (`1.15%` CV). No robust timing outliers were detected.
- The runs have mild early settling (`-0.133` to `-0.214 ms/iteration`). The fixed iterations 10-50 stationary view has per-launch means `141.415/141.863/139.151 ms`; pooled `n=123` is `140.810 +/- 6.568 ms` (`4.66%` CV), with `1.03%` between-launch CV. Moving the cutoff to iteration 15 or 20 leaves the pooled mean within `0.39 ms` and CV within `0.05` percentage points, so iterations 10-50 are the recommended reproducible baseline window.
- Exact timer reconstruction from 15 consecutive all-rank trace intervals matches the driver within `-0.049 +/- 0.031 ms` (range `-0.102` to `+0.005 ms`). The profiled interval splits into a `36.019 +/- 4.955 ms` prior logging tail and `151.332 +/- 3.842 ms` core-to-timer-stop segment. Logging-tail variance is `55.0%` of total trace variance, core variance is `33.1%`, and their covariance contributes `11.9%`.
- The dominant logging variance is not model work or NCCL service. `Timer.elapsed()` restarts the interval timer, then `energy_monitor.lap()` calls NVML and performs a one-element world all-reduce, so that tail is charged to the next logged iteration. Max post-barrier host gap correlates `r=0.824` with logged time. In the detailed trace, late rank 14 spends `31.372 ms` in `nvmlDeviceGetTotalEnergyConsumption`, matching its `31.887 ms` host gap. The energy all-reduce service after the last participant arrives is only `0.049 +/- 0.018 ms`; the late rank moves across nine ranks spanning both healthy replicas over 15 intervals.
- Core variance is model-EP participant readiness, not model-EP transport. Pre-timer-stop SendRecv residency correlates `r=0.850` with core duration. Participant-aligned analysis of all 36 SendRecv operations in both EP8 groups gives `0.171 +/- 0.112 ms` matched service per collective; max-group service has only `0.223 ms` iteration-level standard deviation and `r=0.081` with core duration. Accumulated participant-arrival spread has `3.949 ms` standard deviation and `r=0.855`. Several ordinals and changing latest ranks contribute; no single operation or rank explains it. The accumulated spread is a propagated-skew indicator and must not be interpreted as additive overhead.
- Healthy replicas do exhibit the same class of local delay inside the per-expert grouped-linear launch loop seen previously on reduced rank 9. Detailed steps select rank 1 with a true `7.595 ms` internal grouped-linear launch gap and rank 7 with `8.305 ms`; both are healthy ranks and the delayed rank changes. Those events are absorbed by later synchronization and do not determine final completion: timer-stop skew is only `1.360 +/- 0.202 ms`, rank 15 is last in all 15 low-overhead intervals, and grouped-linear max host time/launch gap correlate only `r=0.480/0.449` with core duration. The traces do not record the underlying `m_splits` values directly, so the supported conclusion is that this class of expert-launch delay can happen on any expert rank, including healthy replicas, but it is not the dominant variance source in this baseline.
- Reproducible artifacts are `scripts/nonuniform/run_lyris_a3b_ep8_baseline_characterization.sh`, `scripts/nonuniform/analyze_ep8_baseline_variance.py`, and `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_baseline_characterization/analysis_2450978.json`. The analyzer preserves per-iteration data, all rank/step metrics, timer boundaries, participant-aligned SendRecv metrics, and per-ordinal latest-rank distributions.

## 2026-07-21 - Healthy EP8 baseline variance characterization prepared

- The rank-9 follow-up localized its repeated grouped-linear delay to host work inside Transformer Engine's per-expert cuBLASLt launch loop: the delayed call has the same 64 expert kernels and nearly identical GPU union as its peer, but a repeatable approximately `1.6 ms` gap before one `cuLaunchKernelEx`. Because this mechanism is driven by local expert `m_splits`, it can affect any expert rank, including ranks in a healthy replica; it is not inherently an NEP or reduced-replica behavior.

- Added `scripts/nonuniform/analyze_ep8_baseline_variance.py`. It reports per-launch and pooled mean, median, sample standard deviation, CV, percentiles, MAD outliers, lag-one correlation, drift, and between-launch variance. Submission and results are pending.

## 2026-07-21 - Final owner-EDP rank-8 delay attribution

- Verified the exact topology from job `2443953`: ranks `0-7` are full replica 0 and ranks `8-11` are reduced replica 1. Rank 8 is therefore a reduced-replica owner; its TP group is `[8, 9]` and its owner-EDP group is `[0, 8]`.
- Rank 8 is not late at the host EDP-launch rendezvous. It enters `nep_split_wait_edp_launch` `1.923 ms` before rank 0 and waits `2.001 ms`; rank 0 reaches the pair barrier last. Native DDP then records the final EDP collective at rank-0-relative `+0.455 ms`, but rank 8's NCCL kernel does not begin until `+3.345 ms`, leaving a `2.890 ms` enqueue-to-device delay. Rank 0's corresponding delay is only `0.087 ms`.
- The device timeline identifies the dependency. Rank 8's TP peer rank 9 launches the two preceding CUDA graphs `7.599/7.628 ms` after rank 8. Rank 8 consequently enters the first TP all-gather at `-1.255 ms`, while rank 9 enters at `+1.014 ms`; both finish at approximately `+1.076 ms`, so `2.269 ms` of rank-8 residency is participant wait. A later TP all-gather similarly starts at `+2.721/+3.124 ms` on ranks 8/9 and completes at `+3.193 ms`. The final EDP kernels on ranks 8 and 9 then both start at `+3.345 ms`. This is the direct reason rank 8 reaches owner EDP after rank 0: its already-enqueued TP graph cannot advance until rank 9 catches up.
- Rank 9's immediate source of skew is one expert `_GroupedLinearBackward` operation, sequence `2725`: its host scope is `8.145 ms` versus `2.029 ms` on rank 8, accounting for `6.116 ms` of the final graph-launch gap. Every reduced rank launches 64 kernels for this operation; rank 9 has less summed kernel residency than rank 8 (`0.790` versus `1.292 ms`), excluding extra expert math as the explanation. The same rank-9 outlier exists in both controls (`10.028/9.110 ms` versus rank-8 `2.167/2.103 ms`) with `6.260/8.104 ms` final graph-launch skew, so host-progress scheduling did not create it.
- The candidate differs by enqueueing EDP while this pre-existing TP skew is unresolved. In both controls, rank 8's final EDP is enqueued only after the TP graph drains and starts within `0.084-0.085 ms`; in the host-progress candidate it is enqueued earlier and waits `2.890 ms`. The remaining internal cause of rank 9's long grouped-linear host scope is not represented by GPU work or CUDA-runtime time in the trace: CUDA runtime calls total only `0.164 ms`, with event-query clusters inside an otherwise opaque Transformer Engine scope. Distinguishing TE host scheduling from OS thread descheduling requires TE-side markers or CPU scheduler/stack profiling; neither is present in the saved trace.

Gather, owner EDP, Scatter, and the completion fence all executed.

Rank-0 useful reshard overlap increased from `6.23/8.13%` in the controls to `20.49%`, primarily because Scatter gained `0.877 ms` of useful overlap. This was a wrapper timeout, not a training failure. Exact retry `2443953` completed all four healthy/A/B/A cases `0:0` on GB200 nodes `lyris[0077-0078,0081,0084]` in `11m27s`, producing 52 traces. Every case completed eight finite iterations with zero skips/NaNs.
- Clean iterations 5-8 for job `2443953` averaged `156.725 ms` healthy, `178.500 ms` current A, `169.400 ms` host-progress B, and `172.875 ms` repeated A. B is `3.58%` faster than the bracketed A mean (`175.688 ms`) but reaches only `92.52%` owner-time parity with healthy, below the required `95%`. Reported throughput averaged approximately `197.15` versus `181.95 TFLOP/s/GPU` for healthy/B (`92.29%`). The two A controls differ by `3.15%`, so the wall-time gain is not large relative to allocation drift.
- All 36 NEP traces have identical ordered collective signatures within every process group on all 12 ranks across A/B/A. Counts, participants, split sizes, dtypes, and payloads match exactly; each case records 432 model-EP all-to-alls, 48 owner-transfer all-to-alls, 24 owner-EDP reductions, and 48 dense-DP coalesced reductions when summed over ranks. The intended cross-process-group interleaving changes because B launches NEP phases earlier.
- The trace confirms that host progress changes scheduling but does not solve exposure. Rank-0 useful reshard overlap increases from `3.316/3.287 ms` (`13.08/12.74%`) in A/A to `7.496 ms` (`24.22%`) in B, driven mainly by Scatter overlap increasing from `0` to `3.909 ms`. Owner EDP remains almost entirely exposed: overlap changes from `0.247/0.243 ms` to only `0.657 ms`. Absolute unhidden Gather+EDP+Scatter time is `22.024/22.517 ms` in A/A versus `23.458 ms` in B, so the candidate makes total exposed reshard approximately `0.94-1.43 ms` worse despite the higher overlap percentage.
- B moves the final owner EDP launch approximately `6.1-8.6 ms` earlier, but full owner rank 0 then starts `3.345 ms` before reduced participant rank 8. That final all-reduce occupies rank 0 for `9.311 ms` (`3.345 ms` participant skew plus `5.966 ms` matched service), versus `4.821/5.335 ms` in the controls. The early SM-based NCCL kernel therefore waits and contends instead of being hidden by useful compute. Model-EP matched service remains stable (`5.811/5.833/5.744 ms`) and dense-DP service does not inflate (`8.062/7.732/8.909 ms`); lower propagated participant-wait residency, rather than lower absolute reshard exposure, explains the candidate's shorter profiled path.
- Acceptance decision: correctness passes and the launch-point hypothesis is confirmed, but the exact workload misses the `>=95%` owner-parity gate and does not reduce absolute exposed reshard. Restored `CASE_POST_GRAPH_HOST_PHASES=0` as the sweep default while retaining the explicit candidate flag and reproducible A/B/A wrapper. No benchmark jobs remain queued or running.

- The controlled fence test compares `fence+no-check -> deferred-fence+no-check -> fence+no-check` on the same nodes. Fenced means are `168.74/172.16 ms`; deferred is `170.30 ms`, so no wall-time speedup is measurable. Both fenced traces have exactly zero Scatter overlap. Deferring only the completion fence makes the first two Scatters start within `0.002 ms` of the next model-EP kernel and overlap `1.733 ms` of useful compute plus `0.985 ms` of model-EP NCCL. Owner EDP remains essentially unhidden (`0.245 ms` useful overlap across `15.179 ms`).
- Source and device timelines identify the remaining cause. Gather is launched when its bucket group becomes ready, but with both post-graph progress modes disabled, `_finish_pending_nep_dispatch_host_phases()` is not called until the next MoE backward pre-hook. That function then launches owner EDP and Scatter before returning. In the deferred trace, after Gather completion, `1.921/4.311/3.481 ms` of useful owner compute passes before EDP starts. Rank 0 is the latest EDP participant in every batch and has zero participant wait, excluding the reduced replica as the delay. The next model-EP is CPU-submitted while EDP is still running but begins only `0.573/0.584 ms` after EDP ends; removing the completion fence therefore unlocks Scatter, not EDP. The final EDP/Scatter batch is also an unavoidable tail unless scheduling/grouping changes create earlier readiness.
- All four cases have identical six owner-transfer and three owner-EDP collectives and identical `1,915,748,352`-element payload totals, so the result is not a collective-count or payload change. Synthetic finite checks add a separate `3.959 -> 0.663/0.667 ms` host-scope delta, but removing them does not remove NCCL exposure and is not used to explain it. Updated the analyzer to infer the NEP staging stream from its pack/copy-back signature rather than hard-coding CUDA stream 80; it reproduces job `2441290` exactly. Local compile/diff checks pass; `uv run isort` is unavailable on the login host, while the job's container isort gate passed for the edited implementation/test imports.


## 2026-07-20 - Clean default top-k 6 proportional slowdown analysis

- Restricted the causal analysis to the logging-clean default `top-k=6` pair from job `2441290`; no other top-k result is used. Healthy EP8/EP8 averages `145.564 ms` and proportional NEP EP8/EP4 averages `167.518 ms` over iterations 6-16, a `21.955 ms` gap and `86.89%` owner parity. The two 11-sample distributions have standard deviations `4.625/7.433 ms`; mean GPU power is effectively identical at `577.55/577.68 W`.
- Audited printed arguments exhaustively. Apart from the intended world/DP/GBS, nonuniform topology/mode, profile-rank list, and output paths, every printed model, graph, DDP, routing, and runtime argument matches. Both cases use the same allocation and map full-replica ranks 0-7 to the same first two nodes at four GPUs per node. Parameter-norm and zero-count logging are disabled, manual GC is outside the measured range, stderr is empty, all traces are complete, and all iterations are finite with zero skips/NaNs.
- Routing is not the cause. Mean per-dispatch rank CV is `0.663%` in healthy full EP8, `0.720%` in NEP full EP8, and `0.542%` in reduced EP4; aggregate rank CV is `0.369/0.382/0.344%`. Mean routed assignments are exactly `24,576` per rank/dispatch in every group, and healthy/NEP full-replica model-EP payload differs by only about `0.07%` from random routing realization.
- The clean unprofiled gap and the profiled iteration are separated deliberately. `ProfilerStep#3` is `214.237/232.444 ms`, only an `18.207 ms` gap, and includes a different post-training logging tail. The owner backward path from its first model-EP kernel to the first dependent post-training TP kernel is `89.595/109.775 ms`, a `20.180 ms` NEP gap that closely matches the unprofiled `21.955 ms` regression.
- The backward gap has an exact interval decomposition on full owner rank 0: NEP adds `6.686 ms` of non-NCCL union and `13.945 ms` of NCCL union, loses `2.383 ms` of NCCL/non-NCCL overlap, and has `2.835 ms` less GPU-idle spacing. `6.686 + 13.945 + 2.383 - 2.835 = 20.179 ms`, matching the measured `20.180 ms` trace gap to rounding.
- NEP Gather + owner EDP + Scatter occupies `25.503 ms` on rank 0. Only `3.321 ms` (`13.0%`) overlaps useful non-NCCL kernels, so `22.182 ms` is exposed on that rank. The six matched owner-transfer all-to-alls require `10.951 ms` of true service. The three owner EDP all-reduces require `13.967 ms`, versus `7.645 ms` for healthy native expert EDP; owner EDP carries exactly twice the healthy per-owner payload (`1,915,748,352` versus `957,874,176` float elements) because gradients for 32 experts are concentrated on each surviving owner instead of 16.
- Global model-EP completion milestones show where this time enters the critical path. Before the first reshard, after backward model-EP operation 23, NEP is `5.259 ms` ahead of healthy when both are normalized to backward entry. At the next model-EP dispatch it is `4.482 ms` behind, a `9.741 ms` swing across the first reshard boundary. The second boundary raises the lag to `12.464 ms`; later model compute recovers part of it, leaving `6.167 ms` at the final model-EP completion, and the final reshard/native drains raise the backward gap to `20.180 ms`.
- Native model-EP transport itself is not slower. Across all 36 matched full-replica collectives, service after the last participant arrives is `5.808 ms` healthy versus `5.879 ms` NEP. Rank-0 model-EP residency grows `28.057 -> 50.351 ms` only because rank 0 arrives earlier than another participant and spins; comparing rank-0 NCCL residency without all-rank alignment double-counts propagated skew and is not a causal transport measurement.
- The NEP dispatch stream adds `9.959 ms` of non-NCCL work: `4.760 ms` across 576 device copies, `2.644 ms` across 192 accumulation adds, `0.998 ms` of buffer fills, `1.466 ms` of gradient-norm kernels, and small checks. Relative to healthy, the owner has `+3.790 ms` DtoD copies, `+2.647 ms` adds, and `+1.014 ms` fills before the post-training boundary; expert GEMM deltas on the full owner are negligible by comparison.
- Native DDP's finite-gradient check is an implementation-level overlap blocker in the synthetic owner EDP group. The first `nep_native_ddp_start_grad_sync` scope lasts `8.590 ms`; `7.783 ms` is `aten::item()` -> `cudaStreamSynchronize` while model-EP and Gather are queued on the NEP path. The autograd thread cannot submit later backward compute during this synchronization. This follows directly from `_ParamAndGradBucketGroup.start_grad_sync()` calling `check_grads()` and `check_grads()` reducing each bucket norm before the asynchronous all-reduce.
- NEP also interferes with existing native dense-DP communication. The final 176,163,552-element dense-DP collective has `4.060 ms` matched service in NEP versus `1.717 ms` healthy. It overlaps the final owner EDP kernel for about `2.31 ms`, almost exactly its `2.343 ms` service inflation; the other three large dense-DP collectives add only about `0.40 ms` combined. The differing six-rank/eight-rank dense-DP topology remains a small unavoidable comparison difference, but it does not explain the final inflation.
- Proportional EP4 fragmentation is real but secondary in this clean pair. Reduced EP4 executes 1,152 expert GEMMs averaging `16.151 ms` total versus 576 and `11.409 ms` on NEP full EP8. However, reduced owners reach the first two owner-EDP rendezvous `2.1-10.8 ms` before full owners. They are only `2.8-4.4 ms` late at the final rendezvous; rank 0's `4.425 ms` host wait is covered by already-running dense-DP NCCL, and the actual final EDP kernel launch spread is `0.632 ms`. Fragmentation therefore is not the primary source of the `21.955 ms` owner regression.
- Superseding conclusion for this clean default-top-k case: the proportional slowdown is primarily exposed Gather/owner-EDP/Scatter work plus NEP packing/check kernels, with loss of useful overlap and about `2.34 ms` of native dense-DP contention. It is not caused by raw model-EP bandwidth, routing imbalance, power/placement drift, or owner expert-GEMM efficiency. The current pre-hook fence orders each completed reshard before the next model-EP boundary, and the synchronous finite check delays host submission, so the available intervening compute hides only about 13% of reshard residency.
- Added `scripts/nonuniform/analyze_topk6_proportional_slowdown.py` to reproduce the timing, backward union accounting, all-rank collective service, model-EP milestones, owner-transfer/EDP payloads, dispatch-stream cost, and useful-overlap measurements. `python3 -m py_compile` and the analyzer run pass. The required `uv run isort` command could not run because `uv` is not installed on this login environment; imports are already in standard-library order.
