- Derived effective expert-GEMM throughput from the all-rank traces. With MBS1, TP2 sequence parallelism, six MoE layers, hidden size 2,688, and expert FFN size 1,856, each GPU executes `1.4713 * top-k` TFLOP of routed expert forward/backward GEMMs per iteration. Dividing this useful work by summed NVJet expert-kernel time gives EP8/EP4 throughput of approximately `405/264`, `648/430`, `770/516`, `777/665`, and `801/787 TFLOP/s` at top-k 2, 4, 6, 8, and 12. Top-k 6 averages four traces across its two A-B-A allocations.
- Because balanced proportional EP8 and EP4 ranks have the same useful expert work per GPU while EP4 launches twice as many kernels, the EP4-to-EP8 effective GEMM-efficiency ratio is allocation-normalized. It is about `65.2%`, `66.3%`, `67.0%` (`65.0-69.1%` across repeats), `85.6%`, and `98.2%` at top-k 2, 4, 6, 8, and 12. The trend is therefore flat and inefficient through top-k 6, then improves sharply as the smaller EP4 GEMMs become long enough to amortize launch/tile inefficiency. This metric is kernel-time-derived, not total-model MFU or a hardware-counter tensor-core utilization measurement.
- Cross-rank alignment corrects the earlier model-EP attribution. At top-k 12, full-EP8 and reduced-EP4 ranks execute the same 36 model-EP kernels and exactly the same `3,171,483,648` recorded input/output elements per rank. Service after the last participant arrives averages `281/288 us` per collective, and reduced model-EP residency is `40.28 ms`, versus `36.33 ms` on the NEP full replica and `44.16/41.65 ms` on the two healthy EP8 replicas. The reduced path is therefore not slower than healthy raw model-EP transport; its extra residency relative to the NEP full replica comes from participant launch skew.
- The dominant measured owner gap is timed parameter-norm logging. The runner enables `--log-params-norm`, and `calc_params_l2_norm()` executes before `training_log()` reads the still-running `interval-time` timer. In this no-optimizer-step benchmark, it falls back to `param.data.float()` for each BF16 parameter. EP8 ranks own 284 included tensors, while EP4 ranks own 476: the exact extra `192` tensors are `6` MoE layers times `16` additional local experts times `2` expert weights. Traces show 284 versus 476 `aten::to` operations and 282 versus 471 direct-copy kernels in the post-backward tail.
- The final one-element `dp_cp` norm reduction exposes that imbalance directly. Full-owner versus last-reduced-rank launch spread is `11.49 ms` for first top-k 2, `13.51 ms` for repeated top-k 2, and `13.80 ms` for top-k 12. The corresponding clean healthy-to-NEP owner gaps are `12.60`, `14.43`, and `13.77 ms`. At top-k 12, rank 10 spends the full `13.80 ms` interval issuing 272 remaining `aten::to` operations; 271 direct-copy kernels occupy `4.35 ms` and the rest is launch/synchronization spacing. This one-for-one A-B-A correlation explains why owner parity stays flat after the expert-GEMM gap closes.
- NEP reshard communication is still substantially exposed in the profiled step, but it is not the differentiator isolated by this top-k endpoint measurement. On rank 0 at top-k 12, Gather/Scatter plus owner EDP has `29.86 ms` union with `3.21 ms` of non-NCCL overlap. Each NEP owner EDP path carries twice the healthy per-rank expert-gradient payload because EP8 gradients are concentrated onto four owners (`7.137` versus `3.568 GiB` in summed trace records). A logging-disabled same-allocation A/B is required before using this workload to claim the remaining core-training parity or reshard overhead.

- Added `scripts/nonuniform/analyze_ep8_ep4_trace_evidence.py` and `NEP_EP8_EP4_TRACE_EVIDENCE.md` to make the all-rank analysis of routing job `2438661` reproducible. The analyzer uses `ProfilerStep#3`, clean iterations 6-8, dispatch metadata, dedicated expert streams, matched 176,160,768-element dense-DP kernels, and interval unions/intersections.
- Balanced proportional EP8 and EP4 ranks each receive exactly 49,152 routed assignments per dispatch on average, with only `0.407%` and `0.397%` per-dispatch rank CV. EP4 nevertheless executes 8,064 expert-stream NVJet kernels per rank versus 4,032 on EP8 because it owns 32 rather than 16 experts/GPU. Mean kernel duration falls `40.430 -> 21.171 us`, while aggregate expert residency changes only `163.014 -> 170.727 ms`.
- Reduced ranks launch the first large dense-DP bucket `141.777 ms` after full ranks. Only `28.344 ms` of this is additional GPU-active union; no-GPU-event spacing grows by `113.353 ms`, stream-wait calls by 1,302, and aggregate CUDA-graph launch API time by `9.725 ms` with the same 280 graph launches. The trace therefore supports finer expert-GEMM granularity plus launch/dependency spacing as the reason proportional token allocation does not guarantee equal runtime; it does not assign every inactive microsecond solely to GEMM launch overhead.
- Fixed bias raises per-dispatch routed-load CV to `41.652%` on EP8 and `26.369%` on EP4. The reduced-versus-full dense-DP arrival lag grows `141.777 -> 291.066 ms`, and early full-rank residency grows `144.484 -> 293.642 ms`, while late reduced-rank residency remains about `2.6 ms`; all participant kernels finish together.
- The waiting kernel is the SM-resident `ncclDevKernel_AllReduce_Sum_f32_RING_LL` with grid 32, block 544, stream 32, and ranks `[0, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10]`. In biased NEP, rank-0 dense-DP/model-EP co-residency is `293.898 ms`; native model-EP residency is `568.962 ms` versus `273.624 ms` in biased healthy, a `295.338 ms` increase despite identical native model-EP operation count and aggregate payload. Non-NCCL residency remains `655.791/659.144 ms`.
- Clean full-owner parity is `93.463%` balanced and `90.321%` biased. Bias therefore worsens NEP through later reduced-rank arrival and SM contention, but cannot explain the balanced proportional regression because balanced routing was already within roughly `0.4%` CV and `1.001x` aggregate max/mean.

## 2026-07-20 - EP8/EP4 batch-ratio parity sweep

- Defined parity for this experiment as full-replica owner-rank iteration wall time relative to a same-allocation healthy EP8/EP8 baseline; aggregate throughput and reduced-rank timing are secondary diagnostics.
- Added `scripts/nonuniform/run_lyris_a3b_ep8_ep4_batch_ratio_sweep.sh`. It holds the full EP8 replica at MBS2 x 7 microbatches and runs a healthy EP8/EP8 control plus EP8/EP4 cases whose reduced replica uses 4, 5, 6, 7, or 8 microbatches. These are below proportional, proportional, and above proportional, with true GBS 72, 76, 80, 84, and 88 respectively.
- Every case uses the same 14-stage `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E` workload, 128 experts, TP2/ETP1, forced load balancing, no optimizer step, local CUDA graphs, full Gather -> native EDP -> Scatter, and all-rank PyTorch traces on iterations 3-4. Iterations 6-8 provide clean timing. Shell syntax and direct-rank dry runs pass; rank 0 always maps to seven microbatches and reduced owner rank 8 maps to the requested 4-8 count.
- Four-node/segment-4 test-only estimates were effectively immediate on all GB200/GB300 queues. Selected faster GB300 hardware and submitted sweep job `2438213` to `gb300-backfill`; it allocated `theia[0027-0030]` immediately with a 30-minute allocation and six-minute per-case guards. The mounted implementation is frozen until the job reaches a terminal state.
- Job `2438213` was canceled after `6m21s` and recorded `FAILED 124:0` because the healthy case exhausted its six-minute guard before iteration 1. There was no Python/NCCL/model failure: cold container/model startup consumed about five minutes, and historical traces show the first two local-graph iterations require another roughly 96 and 49 seconds. Increased only the per-case guard to ten minutes, the allocation to 40 minutes, and the graceful exit duration to nine minutes; training remains eight iterations per case.
- Refreshed estimates for the corrected request; GB300-backfill was about 20 minutes earlier than GB200-backfill and effectively immediate. Submitted corrected sweep job `2438260`, which allocated `theia[0237-0240]` immediately.
- Job `2438260` completed all six cases `0:0` in `19m51s` on `theia[0237-0240]`. Every measured iteration was finite with zero skips/NaNs, and all 16 healthy plus 12 traces for each NEP case were written. Clean iteration 6-8 owner-critical-path means were `896.67 ms` healthy, `901.57 ms` at reduced rounds 4, `905.27 ms` at 5, `902.20 ms` at 6, `967.90 ms` at proportional 7, and `1059.63 ms` at above-proportional 8. Full-owner parity was therefore `99.46%`, `99.05%`, `99.39%`, `92.64%`, and `84.62%` respectively.
- The proportional threshold is controlled by reduced-replica arrival, not added owner-transfer volume. At reduced rounds 7, full rank 0 launches the first 176,160,768-element dense-DP bucket at about `+837.8 ms` and occupies NCCL for `117.9 ms`; reduced ranks join about `115 ms` later. At rounds 6, reduced ranks arrive about `25 ms` before the full owner and owner parity returns to `99.39%`. Above proportional at rounds 8, full rank 0 launches the same bucket at `+834.6 ms` and occupies NCCL for `258.2 ms`, while reduced ranks 8/10 join only at `+1089-1091 ms`. Once the reduced replica is early enough (rounds 4-6), further reducing its work does not improve the full owner, which plateaus near the healthy wall time.
- Added explicit routing controls to the shared Lyris a3b runner. The matched follow-up compares healthy and proportional NEP under the current forced-random balanced router and under a fixed shared expert bias (`--moe-router-force-biased=-1.0`), with all other model, batch, graph, communication, and all-rank profiling settings held constant. This will measure whether expert imbalance moves the proportional parity threshold.
- Four-node estimates were `10:16:36` GB200, `10:16:39` GB200-backfill, `10:18:42` GB300, and `10:16:50` GB300-backfill. Submitted the earliest GB200 request as job `2438661`; it is pending for priority with `StartTime=Unknown`, and queue polling is every five minutes.
- Dispatch metadata confirms that the current forced-random mode is already very closely balanced. Across the completed sweep, per-dispatch routed-token CV is about `0.36-0.43%`; the healthy same-allocation control in job `2438661` is `0.43%`, and its per-rank aggregate routed-token max/mean is only `1.001x`. The sharp proportional/above-proportional regression is therefore not random expert-load skew. The biased cases intentionally introduce a much stronger, repeatable contrast.
- Routing-sensitivity job `2438661` completed all four cases `0:0` in `12m25s` on `lyris[0219,0221-0222,0224]`. All 32 measured iterations were finite with zero skips/NaNs, and all 16/12/16/12 requested rank traces were written. Clean iteration 6-8 means were `952.23 ms` balanced healthy, `1018.83 ms` balanced proportional NEP, `1141.23 ms` biased healthy, and `1263.53 ms` biased proportional NEP. Full-owner parity is `93.46%` when balanced and `90.32%` when biased. Forced bias slows healthy by `19.85%`, NEP by `24.02%`, and increases the NEP owner overhead from `6.99%` to `10.72%`.
- The bias produced the intended routing contrast. Balanced per-dispatch EP-rank token CV is `0.40-0.43%`; biased EP8 CV is `41.66%` with mean per-dispatch max/mean `1.777x`, while biased EP4 CV is `26.37%` with max/mean `1.317x`. The sequence load-balancing metric moves from approximately `1.00` to `1.56`.
- Trace causality is explicit. In balanced NEP, full ranks launch the first 176,160,768-element dense-DP bucket at about `+875 ms`, reduced ranks join at `+1017-1018 ms`, and the full-owner kernel resides for about `145 ms`. With biased routing, those times are `+1075 ms` and `+1366-1367 ms`, so owner residency grows to `294 ms`. Direct rank-0 dense-DP/model-EP co-residency grows from `6.26 ms` balanced to `293.90 ms` biased; model-EP residency grows from `273.62 ms` in biased healthy to `568.96 ms` in biased NEP, almost exactly the added co-resident wait. Non-NCCL residency remains `659.14/655.79 ms`, and NEP owner-transfer residency remains `11.54 ms`. Expert skew therefore worsens NEP by making the reduced replica later and allowing its waiting dense-DP kernel to contend with native model-EP work, but it is not the cause of the already-balanced proportional regression.
- EP8/EP4 conclusion before scaling: on the 14-stage MBS2 workload, the current proportional point (seven reduced rounds, 50% aggregate reduced/full work) misses owner parity because EP4's 32 experts/GPU fragment the same nominal per-GPU work more than EP8's 16 experts/GPU. The operational condition for parity is that reduced ranks reach shared dense-DP buckets no later than the full owner. Six reduced rounds arrive about `25 ms` early and reach `99.39%` parity; seven arrive `115-143 ms` late and reach `92.64-93.46%`; eight arrive about `256 ms` late and reach `84.62%`. No EP16 job will be launched until these EP8/EP4 results are reported and reviewed.

## 2026-07-16 - Native dense path and three expert groups

- Saved the complete tracked pre-change diff at `/tmp/nep_pre_deferred_edp_finish_20260716.patch` before changing the native EDP completion lifecycle.
- Replaced the per-Scatter native `finish_grad_sync()` call with a CUDA-stream dependency on the outstanding native EDP `Work`. Scatter is now ordered after EDP on device without clearing the native handle; the custom expert bucket group's final DDP drain calls native `finish_grad_sync()` once after all NEP Gather/Scatter work has drained. Added reset-time checks for undrained native state and focused single/grouped lifecycle tests.
- Audited the multistream runtime settings. The launchers explicitly use `NCCL_LAUNCH_ORDER_IMPLICIT=1`, `TORCH_NCCL_BLOCKING_WAIT=0`, `CUDA_DEVICE_MAX_CONNECTIONS=32`, and `HIGH_PRIORITY_STREAM_GROUPS=ep`. On CUDA 13.2/NCCL 2.30, implicit launch ordering permits overlap across communicators while preserving a consistent launch order; the nonblocking PyTorch wait setting prevents the stream dependency from becoming a CPU-blocking wait. Blackwell does not require the CUDA connection setting, but retaining 32 is compatible with Megatron's MoE-overlap convention.
- Submitted GB200-backfill cached-container preflight `2406774`; it completed `0:0` in `1m19s` on `lyris0141`. Isort check, Python compilation, shell/static checks, and all 79 focused NEP tests passed (`79 passed` in `1.18s`). NeMo 26.06 provides PyTorch `2.12.0a0` and an `_CoalescingManager` whose `wait()` forwards to each NCCL Work's `wait()` rather than exposing `block_current_stream()` directly, so the distributed profiler gate will measure both the `nep_native_ddp_order_scatter_stream` host annotation and GPU ordering before accepting the change.
- Scheduler estimates for the two-layer EP8/EP4 checksum gate favored GB300-backfill over both GB200 partitions and GB300 main. Compared with full-Scatter job `2406986`, this removes `3.78 ms` of owner-rank and `16.43 ms` of follower-rank Scatter kernel residency across four profiled expert buckets without changing EDP count or shape.
- Exact four-node estimates favored GB200-backfill at `16:10 PDT`, followed by GB300-backfill at `16:11`, GB200 main at `17:25`, and GB300 main at `19:16`. Submitted same-allocation job `2407472` on GB200-backfill: healthy EP8/DP2 followed by intentionally incorrect skip-Scatter EP8/EP4 on the exact `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E` 12-slot workload, seven iterations, clean steps 6-7, and profiler ranks 0/4/8 on steps 3-4. Monitoring remains at five-minute intervals.
- Exact skip-Scatter A/B `2407472` completed both cases `0:0` on `lyris[0064-0067]`. Clean steps 6-7 averaged `135.45 ms` / `227.65 TFLOP/s/GPU` healthy versus `171.00 ms` / `180.55 TFLOP/s/GPU` skip-Scatter NEP. The intentionally incorrect candidate is `35.55 ms` (`26.25%`) slower, reaching only `79.21%` latency parity and `79.31%` throughput parity. Its raw latency is just `1.50 ms` below the prior full-Scatter NEP result (`172.50 ms` in job `2407215`), while this allocation healthy baseline is `6.15 ms` faster; normalized parity therefore does not improve (`82.09% -> 79.21%`).
- Target traces again prove Scatter was removed: ranks 0/4 have six Gather-direction owner-transfer kernels and zero return-direction kernels, rank 4 receives no copy-back, ranks 0/8 retain six owner EDP all-reduces, and no Scatter rendezvous annotation appears. On critical owner rank 0 over two profiled steps, owner Gather residency is `13.85 ms`, while expert-DP residency is nearly unchanged from healthy (`29.35 -> 30.14 ms`). The slowdown instead tracks native NCCL inflation: model-EP grows `42.18 -> 78.40 ms`, dense-DP gradient all-reduce grows `17.77 -> 40.60 ms`, and the additional DP all-reduces grow `4.35 -> 20.24 ms`. Their combined inflation is `37.48 ms/step`, close to the profiled wall gap of `39.30 ms/step`. Scatter was not the dominant critical-path cost; the remaining Gather/EDP schedule still contends with or serializes Megatron native collectives.
- Deeper cross-rank trace alignment shows the native inflation is primarily displaced synchronization wait, not direct SM contention. Rank-0 model-EP has `0.0 ms` direct overlap with NEP Gather/EDP, yet rank-0/rank-4 launch skew within the identical EP group `[0..7]` grows from `0.661 ms` healthy to `1.364 ms` NEP on average and from `7.696` to `14.633 ms` maximum; collective completion differs by only about `6 us`. In the clearest event, rank 4 launches `14.633 ms` before rank 0 and its NCCL kernel remains resident `14.850 ms` versus rank 0s `0.204 ms`, ending together. Dense-DP sampled-rank start spread similarly grows `1.947 -> 3.637 ms` average and `4.358 -> 9.888 ms` maximum while completion remains aligned within `29 us`.
- Mechanism: owners execute Gather plus EDP while followers execute only Gather. The normal Scatter barrier/all-to-all is also the post-EDP rendezvous that realigns them. Skip-Scatter removes that rendezvous, and the per-layer completion fence lets each local compute stream continue as soon as its role-specific completion event fires. The next model-EP or dense-DP collective becomes the first common rendezvous, so early ranks spin inside native NCCL until slower owners/replicas arrive. Only `3.077 ms` of dense NCCL overlaps NEP traffic over two rank-0 profiled steps, making direct EDP resource contention secondary to this arrival-skew transfer.
- Owner-only accounting corrects the aggregate interpretation. Full-replica owner rank 0 averages `177.734 ms` healthy versus `219.470 ms` skip-Scatter across the two profiled steps: `+41.736 ms` or `23.48%` slower. Reduced-replica owner rank 8 averages `177.690 -> 219.456 ms` (`+41.766 ms`, `23.51%`), with both owner traces ending together. The clean rank-0 acceptance steps show `135.45 -> 171.00 ms` (`+35.55 ms`, `26.25%`); profiler perturbation accounts for the different absolute gap.
- Rank-0 direct owner work is material: Gather is `6.924 ms/step`, owner-side DtoD copies add about `3.193 ms/step`, and the extra accumulation/scaling/fill kernels add about `4.62 ms/step`; owner EDP itself rises only `0.396 ms/step` relative to healthy expert-DP. Native model-EP residency rises `18.108 ms/step` and dense-DP residency `19.365 ms/step`, but these are not simply additive with direct work. Cross-rank alignment gives owner-wait lower bounds: rank 0 waits for profiled follower rank 4 inside model-EP for `22.163 ms/step` versus `10.640 ms` healthy (`+11.523 ms`), and waits for later sampled dense-DP participants for `11.086` versus `2.671 ms/step` (`+8.415 ms`). Thus at least about `19.94 ms/step` of the full owners regression is actual rank-0 waiting, not residency charged only to an early follower. Unprofiled ranks 1-3/5-7 may account for additional wait.
- Reduced owner rank 8 is not the throughput limiter: its model-EP residency falls `17.714 -> 10.168 ms/step` and EDP falls `20.418 -> 16.857 ms/step`; it has no Gather. Its equal step wall time comes from dense/global synchronization with the slower full replica. The critical optimization target remains full owner rank 0 and its peer owners in ranks 1-3.
- Exact sampled causal chain: for dense-DP bucket 4, ranks 0/4 launch at `+0.000/+1.238 ms` but reduced owner rank 8 launches at `+9.888 ms`; durations are `11.977/10.677/2.066 ms` and completion aligns. Before joining, rank 8 spends `7.513 ms` in ordinary reduced-replica GPU compute plus `0.478 ms` in TP communication. Rank 4 is consequently still resident in that dense all-reduce when full owner rank 0 launches the following model-EP collective; rank 4 joins `8.018 ms` later, so rank 0s model-EP kernel lasts `8.026 ms` versus rank 4s `0.010 ms` and they finish together. The wait therefore propagates reduced-owner compute -> mixed dense-DP group `[0,2,4,6,8,10]` -> full-replica follower rank 4 -> model-EP group `[0..7]` -> full owner rank 0.
- Other sampled rank-0 model-EP waits have the same structural cause but a different immediate late peer state: rank 4 is still executing prior MoE expert GEMMs and token sorting for gaps of `6.791`, `5.794`, `4.502`, `4.142`, and `3.103 ms`. Rank 4s total local compute changes little (`69.577 -> 70.204 ms/step`), so those are primarily phase shifts propagated from earlier synchronization, not substantial extra follower work. Reduced owner rank 8 does have materially more local residency (`70.230 -> 79.457 ms/step` non-collective kernels and `0.929 -> 7.199 ms/step` DtoD copies): EP4 places 32 experts/GPU instead of 16 and uses half the replica batch, preserving nominal tokens/GPU but fragmenting them across twice as many smaller expert operations and adding owner-buffer work.
- A deeper operator-level check corrects the phrase "ordinary reduced-replica GPU compute" in the sampled dense-bucket chain. The `7.513 ms` immediate window on rank 8 is primarily vocabulary-parallel cross-entropy forward/backward plus output-projection backward and its TP all-gather/reduce-scatter; dense bucket 1 contains `module.output_layer.weight`. Those kernels are not materially slower on rank 8: cross-entropy forward spans `5.649 ms` on rank 8 versus `5.650/5.690 ms` on ranks 0/4, and cross-entropy backward sums `1.412 ms` versus `1.414/1.425 ms`. Rank 8 instead starts the loss GPU work at `59.540 ms`, versus `50.826/51.845 ms`, because it is already `7.7-8.7 ms` behind after the EP4 forward. Before loss, rank 8 emits `1,075` GPU kernels versus `893` on either sampled full-replica rank and has `38.766 ms` of all-stream idle/dependency gaps versus `24.158/28.589 ms`; this is consistent with 32 experts/GPU fragmenting the same nominal token load across more small expert operations. Reducing the reduced-replica batch is therefore not expected to remove this bottleneck; MBS is already 1 and GBS 6 gives exactly one microbatch per each of the six data-parallel lanes, so reducing it further would require idle/uneven lanes and would make expert GEMMs smaller.
- EP8/EP6 is a valid larger-reduced-replica experiment with `TP=2`: use nonuniform topology `4 3`, 14 GPUs, MBS1/GBS7, and compare against healthy topology `4 4`, 16 GPUs, MBS1/GBS8. NEP placement requires the expert count to divide both 8 and 6, hence a multiple of `lcm(8, 6) = 24`; `120` is the closest valid count to the current 128. This gives 15 experts/GPU on EP8 and 20 on EP6, versus 16/32 in the current 128-expert EP8/EP4 case, and should substantially reduce reduced-replica expert fragmentation. Both healthy and NEP cases must use 120 experts for a valid performance comparison.
- Kernel-type attribution strengthens, but does not fully prove, the fragmentation diagnosis. Before loss, ranks 0/4 each execute 227 NVJet GEMMs while reduced EP4 rank 8 executes 419: exactly 192 additional GEMMs, matching `16` additional local experts times six MoE slots times two expert linears. Every other major category has the same count except two additional fills and 12 fewer miscellaneous kernels. Rank-8 GEMMs average only `18.308 us` versus `29.069/29.700 us` on ranks 0/4, and their aggregate residency rises by only about `1.0 ms` (`7.671` versus `6.599/6.742 ms`), so the remaining pre-loss delay is chiefly launch/dependency spacing around those extra small GEMMs. This makes expert fragmentation a high-confidence cause of rank 8 sampled `7.7-8.7 ms` lag, but not yet a proven explanation for the entire `35.55 ms` job-level regression; owner Gather/EDP work and unprofiled-rank synchronization remain contributors. The controlled EP8/EP6 A/B is needed to measure how much job-level parity this removes.
- The workload already uses `--moe-router-force-load-balancing`, but this is statistical rather than exact balancing: `RandomSTE` replaces logits with rank-specific normal random values before top-k routing. Perfect deterministic balance could reduce residual token-count and EP-rank straggler variance, but it would not remove the structural fragmentation observed here. EP4 would still launch 32 local experts versus 16 on EP8, including the measured 192 additional expert GEMMs across six MoE slots. Exact balance is therefore expected to provide a secondary improvement at most; the larger reduced EP directly reduces the launch count. All future profiler experiments will capture every participating rank.
- Work assignment should follow measured replica throughput rather than GPU count alone. The current EP8/EP4 assignment is four versus two samples (`2:1`), which gives equal nominal samples and routed tokens per GPU. Using the sampled pre-loss spans (`50.6-51.6 ms` full for four samples versus `59.1 ms` reduced for two) gives a rough throughput-weighted target of about `2.3:1` full-to-reduced work. Prefer adding full-replica samples to consume otherwise idle capacity rather than reducing the already MBS1 reduced work, which makes expert GEMMs still smaller and discards global throughput. Either direction requires heterogeneous per-replica microbatch scheduling plus sample-count-aware gradient normalization; the current static Megatron schedule cannot directly express the fractional target. EP8/EP6 remains the simpler structural experiment before adding that scheduler.
- Only ranks 0/4/8 were profiled. They prove rank 4 and rank 8 are specific late participants in the sampled owner-critical waits, but cannot identify whether unprofiled owners 1-3 or followers 5-7 are later on other collectives. A complete peer attribution requires traces from ranks 0-10 in the relevant TP lane.

The resulting `80.54%` parity is worse than the prior 12-group `88.08%`; three groups are rejected as a performance improvement even though the native dense-path restoration and grouped execution are functioning as designed.

## 2026-07-16 - NTP-style native DDP refactor

- Refactored the ordered Approach A pipeline around the NTP lifecycle: the existing owner-layout all-to-all Gather remains the pre-sync reshard, a cached synthetic `_ParamAndGradBucketGroup` now owns gradient scaling and the owner `ep_dp` synchronization through native Megatron `start_grad_sync()`/`finish_grad_sync()`, and the existing all-to-all Scatter remains the post-sync reshard.
- Removed manual source-bucket scaling before Gather so native DDP applies the expert gradient scaling factor exactly once to the assembled owner tensor. The scheduler still owns readiness and collective order; owner ranks call native DDP after Gather and finish it immediately before Scatter, while non-owner ranks skip the native EDP bucket and retain the matching transfer sequence.
- Added native lifecycle profiler annotations and reset-time checks for unfinished native reductions. Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass; focused unit and distributed validation are next.
- Submitted one-node GB300-backfill container preflight `2406033` using cached NeMo 26.06. It runs Black check, compilation, the full focused NEP unit-test file, and the nonblocking slot check; initial state is `Priority` with `StartTime=Unknown`, so monitoring uses the required five-minute cadence.
- Preflight `2406033` failed before compilation or tests because the repo requires Black 24 while NeMo 26.06 contains Black 26.3.1. No source was reformatted. Submitted direct compile/full-test retry `2406053`, which allocated `theia0192` at `13:03:20 PDT` and intentionally bypasses only the incompatible Black check.
- Direct preflight `2406053` completed `0:0` in `1m35s`; compilation passed and all 73 focused NEP tests passed in `1.41s`. Parameterized the two-layer checksum harness so its reference and candidate can select different DDP approaches without changing the default behavior.
- Submitted three-node/segment-3 GB300-backfill checksum job `2406070`: P2P reference versus refactored one-chunk NCCL Approach A, both EP8/EP4 (`4 2` at TP2), MBS1/GBS24, two layers, two iterations, forced load balancing, no optimizer, and all-rank topology-aware gradient fingerprints. Initial state is `Priority` with `StartTime=Unknown`; monitoring is every five minutes.
- Job `2406070` allocated `theia[0247,0249-0250]`, but its P2P reference never completed the first two-layer iteration after lazy pair-communicator creation and hit the six-minute per-case timeout. The refactored NCCL case never started; the timeout is isolated from this change because the P2P class is untouched.
- Located accepted one-chunk EP8/EP4 checksum ground truth from job `2404911`. Submitted NCCL-only retry `2406109` with the identical two-layer model, EP8/EP4 topology, MBS1/GBS24, seed, forced load balancing, no optimizer, and checksum fields; it will be compared directly against all 12 saved rank artifacts. Initial state is `Priority`, `StartTime=Unknown`.
- Job `2406109` later received a `16:01 PDT` GB300 estimate. Test-only estimates were `16:41` on GB300 main versus immediate on GB200-backfill, so the pending GB300 request was canceled and identical replacement `2406131` submitted on GB200. It allocated `lyris[0067-0069]` at `13:24:19 PDT`.
- Going forward, test both GB200 and GB300 scheduling before every submission and choose whichever starts sooner. Performance comparisons must still run all compared cases on one hardware type in the same allocation.
- GB200 correctness job `2406131` completed `0:0` in `1m57s`. Both iterations were finite, debug logs show ordered Gather -> native DDP owner sync -> Scatter on both EDP participants, and all 14 checksum fields for all 12 ranks match accepted one-chunk job `2404911` exactly (`0.000e+00` maximum relative delta).
- For the exact 14-stage A/B, initial test-only estimates favored GB200-backfill, but real job `2406139` had no schedulable nodes. Updated estimates favored GB200 main over both GB300 partitions, so `2406139` was canceled and replacement `2406146` submitted on GB200 main. It allocated `lyris[0045,0047,0049-0050]` at `13:29:35 PDT` and runs healthy EP8/DP2 then one-chunk native-DDP EP8/EP4 in one allocation, with rank 0/4/8 profile steps 3-4 and clean steps 6-7.
- Job `2406146` completed `0:0` in `5m34s`. Clean steps 6-7 averaged `134.15 ms` and `229.75 TFLOP/s/GPU` for healthy EP8/DP2 versus `152.30 ms` and `202.40 TFLOP/s/GPU` for native-DDP NEP EP8/EP4. NEP reaches `88.08%` latency parity and `88.10%` throughput parity, a `13.53%` latency overhead; all iterations were finite with zero skipped or NaN iterations.
- The traces verify the intended NTP-style participant mapping. Full and reduced owners (ranks 0 and 8) each execute 12 native `start_grad_sync()`/`finish_grad_sync()` lifecycles per profiled step and carry `ep_dp` `allreduce_coalesced` kernels; follower rank 4 executes neither. Ranks 0 and 4 carry the owner-transfer all-to-alls, while reduced owner rank 8 does not. This matches Gather -> native EDP -> Scatter ownership.
- On performance-critical rank 0, healthy expert-DP averaged `7.385 ms/step` with `0.662 ms` direct non-NCCL overlap. Native-DDP NEP expert-DP averaged `18.339 ms/step` with `2.029 ms` overlap, and owner transfer averaged `13.142 ms/step` with `0.705 ms` overlap. Exposed owner transfer plus incremental exposed EDP is therefore about `12.437 + 9.587 = 22.024 ms/step`, consistent with the `18.15 ms` clean and `25.62 ms` profiled wall-clock gaps.
- Native model-EP and dense-DP residency are not systematically inflated across sampled ranks; rank-0 model-EP is lower in NEP than healthy over the two traces. The refactor successfully moves EDP correctness/scaling/async-handle ownership into Megatron DDP, but does not by itself move the existing late ordered Gather/EDP/Scatter launch points. Direct reshard overlap remains low, so performance is essentially the prior ordered-path regime rather than the target `>=95%` parity.
- Updated `analyze_dlcluster_min_nep.py` with `--iter-start` and an explicit empty-selection error so intentionally short profiler runs can use the repository analyzer without its previous hard-coded iteration-13 assumption.
- Bucket inspection for job `2406146` found `--ddp-num-buckets 16`. This resolved to a `154,354,044`-element target for NEP and `94,486,908` elements for healthy; actual FP32 expert source buckets were seven mostly `154,656,768`-element (`590 MiB`) buckets for NEP and eleven mostly `94,789,632`-element (`361.6 MiB`) buckets for healthy. Several source buckets already cross adjacent MoE-layer boundaries.
- Approach A currently discards those source bucket boundaries when it rebuilds expert groups by logical parameter slot. The native owner path therefore reduced 12 separate `159,645,696`-element FP32 tensors (`609 MiB` each) per step, exactly `fc1` and `fc2` for each of six MoE layers. Changing `--ddp-num-buckets` alone cannot coalesce these NEP reductions.
- To test multi-layer bucketing, NEP must aggregate consecutive logical slots into owner-layout native DDP buckets, retain per-slot Gather readiness, launch one EDP reduction when the aggregate bucket becomes ready, and Scatter its slots afterward. Earlier aggregate buckets can overlap with remaining backward compute; an all-model bucket would instead become ready only at the backward tail and expose all of its communication, so the useful experiment is a small number of multi-layer buckets rather than one unlimited bucket.
- Clarified that the per-slot regrouping is an implementation mistake relative to the requirement to use Megatron native DDP, not an intended NEP property. The refactor reused Approach A's old slot builder and called native `_ParamAndGradBucketGroup.start_grad_sync()` on a new synthetic tensor, but it did not preserve Megatron's source bucket objects, bucket boundaries, parameter readiness, or `param_to_bucket_group` mapping. The correction must follow NTP more literally: wrap the existing expert bucket groups, create a participant-consistent owner-layout view for each native bucket, let native bucket readiness trigger EDP sync, and launch post-sync Scatter from that same bucket lifecycle.
- Megatron DDP already keeps expert and non-expert parameters separate. Parameters with `param.allreduce == False` are placed in `expert_parallel_buffers`, partitioned into `expert_parallel_bucket_groups`, and reduced on the expert-DP group; all other parameters use `buffers`/`bucket_groups` and the DP-CP group. In job `2406146`, NEP had three non-expert bucket groups and seven expert bucket groups. The native-bucket NEP correction should wrap only the seven expert groups and leave dense/shared/router synchronization unchanged.
- Current Approach A does not in fact leave non-expert DDP behavior fully unchanged. Before parent DDP construction it MAX-synchronizes the locally derived bucket threshold across the NEP DP topology, which changed job `2406146` from the healthy `94,486,908`-element threshold/four non-expert groups to `154,354,044` elements/three non-expert groups. It also overrides the backward post-hook so native non-expert buckets only record readiness; their `start_grad_sync()` calls are deferred until `finish_grad_sync()` after NEP expert work drains. Their bucket objects and DP-CP communicator remain native, but their partitioning and overlap schedule are changed. The corrected NTP-style implementation should remove both effects for non-expert buckets unless a participant-consistency requirement is demonstrated specifically for them.

## 2026-07-16 - Ordered-split chunk granularity

- Decisive shared-path sweep `2405155` completed `0:0` in `14m15s` on four GB300 nodes in segment 4. Clean steps 6-7 averaged `132.45 ms`/`232.70 TFLOP/s/GPU` healthy, `147.35 ms`/`209.10 TFLOP/s/GPU` at one chunk, `157.00 ms`/`196.30 TFLOP/s/GPU` at two chunks, and `165.30 ms`/`186.45 TFLOP/s/GPU` at four chunks. Relative to one chunk, two chunks are `6.55%` slower and four chunks are `12.18%` slower; latency parity with healthy falls from `89.89%` to `84.36%` and `80.13%`. Every measured iteration was finite with zero skipped or NaN iterations.
- The rank-0 traces explain the regression. Total NEP payload is identical, but `ep_dp` kernel count rises `24 -> 48 -> 96` and owner-transfer count rises `48 -> 96 -> 192` over two profiled steps for one, two, and four chunks. Global NCCL residency rises `65.565 -> 70.494 -> 75.329 ms`, while NCCL/non-NCCL overlap falls `4.710 -> 4.405 -> 3.887 ms` (`7.18% -> 6.25% -> 5.16%`). NEP host collective/wait annotation time rises from `8.662` to `16.242` and `31.160 ms` over the two steps.
- Per-step compute union remains essentially fixed near `58.4 ms`, but average GPU-active time grows by `5.4 ms` at two chunks and `10.6 ms` at four chunks, while average GPU-idle gaps grow by `7.1 ms` and `14.5 ms`. The ordered scheduler still queues every chunk in the same bulk reshard phases, so smaller chunks do not launch earlier relative to useful backward compute. They only fragment messages, multiply ProcessGroup/NCCL launches, reduce collective efficiency, and leave the launch gaps on the critical path. Native `ep` and `dp_cp` residency is not inflated; the regression is localized to NEP chunk fragmentation. Keep the one-chunk setting for this implementation.
- Submitted decisive GB300 four-node/segment-4 sweep `2405155`: healthy, original-unset ordered split, then target 1, 2, and 4 through the shared range scheduler. This directly checks original versus target-1 performance identity before evaluating smaller chunks. It initially waits on `Priority` with `StartTime=Unknown`; monitoring is every five minutes.
- Revised one-chunk gate `2404911` completed `0:0` in `7m03s` on `theia[0223-0224,0233]`, segment 3. All five focused tests passed, including exact equality of unset-original and target-1 scheduler inputs. The two-layer target-1 ordered-split run completed two finite iterations with no fallback and every gradient checksum field matched stable exactly.
- Initial performance sweep `2402617` completed `0:0` in `14m12s` on `theia[0187,0190-0192]`. Clean steps 6-7 were `128.65 ms` healthy, `165.05 ms` for target 1, `163.15 ms` for target 2, `165.85 ms` for target 4, and `184.90 ms` for target 8. The target-1 control was unexpectedly slower than the prior ordered-split result despite similar healthy latency, so this sweep is diagnostic rather than accepted.

## 2026-07-16 - EDP CGA scheduling

- GB300 same-allocation ordered-split compute-window job `2401831` completed all four cases `0:0` in `11m52s` on `theia[0225-0227,0230]`. Every measured iteration was finite with zero skipped/NaN iterations. MBS1 clean steps 6-7 averaged `125.85 ms` healthy and `147.25 ms` NEP: `21.40 ms` absolute slowdown, `17.00%` relative latency overhead, and `85.47%` parity. MBS2 clean steps averaged `165.05 ms` healthy and `192.40 ms` NEP: `27.35 ms` absolute slowdown, `16.57%` relative overhead, and `85.78%` parity. The larger compute window improves clean parity by only `0.32` percentage points and does not materially close the gap.
- Rank-0 traces show that MBS2 does create more direct concurrency. Owner-transfer overlap rises from `0.50/10.22 ms` (`4.9%`) at MBS1 to `1.29/10.69 ms` (`12.0%`) at MBS2. EDP overlap rises from `1.49/14.91 ms` (`10.0%`) to `3.06/15.80 ms` (`19.4%`). Relative to healthy expert-DP, the combined exposed owner-transfer plus incremental EDP cost falls only from about `16.03` to `14.97 ms/step`, roughly `1.06 ms` hidden by doubling token compute. No further scheduler or workload experiment was submitted pending user discussion.
- User fixed the optimization scope at the validated ordered-split scheduler: no further algorithm variants. The objective is now to overlap the full owner's owner-transfer all-to-alls and EDP work. Removed the unvalidated worker-assisted readiness edit and restored the previously tested opt-in post-graph fixture; the default ordered-split path remains unchanged.
- Prepared a same-allocation compute-window A/B on GB300 because the GB200 inventory remains unavailable. It runs exact `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E` ordered split at MBS1/GBS8 healthy and GBS6 NEP, then MBS2/GBS16 healthy and GBS12 NEP. Doubling MBS while scaling GBS keeps the microbatch schedule and fixed-size gradient reshard count unchanged but doubles token compute, directly testing whether the existing launch points can hide more owner communication. Only rank 0 is profiled, with steps 6-7 reserved for clean timing.
- Corrected the 14-stage ordered-split attribution to use the full-replica owner rank as the performance-critical comparison. Early NCCL residency on follower and reduced-owner ranks is not itself an end-to-end cost. In job `2389611`, structured rank-0 profiler-step wall clock averaged `196.97 ms` healthy versus `207.66 ms` NEP; the cleaner unprofiled window remains `140.48 ms` versus `158.02 ms`, a `17.54 ms` gap.
- On rank 0, NEP owner-transfer all-to-alls occupy `11.94 ms/step` and overlap non-NCCL compute by only `0.65 ms`, leaving about `11.29 ms/step` exposed. NEP EDP occupies `15.49 ms/step` with `1.40 ms` overlapped, versus healthy expert-DP at `7.43 ms/step` with `0.65 ms` overlapped; the incremental exposed EDP cost is therefore about `7.31 ms/step`.
- The two owner-critical deltas total about `18.60 ms/step`, closely matching the clean `17.54 ms` slowdown. NEP's canonical full-replica owner reduces exactly twice the healthy rank-0 expert-gradient payload per step (`1,915,748,352` versus `957,874,176` float elements) because it first gathers the paired full-EP shard. Host Gloo waits and peer early-arrival residency are scheduling manifestations and must not be added again. The accepted diagnosis is insufficient overlap of the full owner's additional reshard plus doubled EDP work, not follower waiting by itself.
- EDP-CGA1 profile `2396467` completed healthy and NEP cases `0:0` in `6m31s`. Clean steps 6-7 averaged `77.00 ms` healthy versus `77.60 ms` NEP, reaching `99.23%` latency parity on the four-stage `MEME` proxy. This is the best same-allocation result in the current cycle.
- The profiled steps do not yet establish a mechanism: sampled NEP EDP residency/overlap remained mixed (`10.22 ms`/`8.1%` on rank 0 and `17.13 ms`/`10.7%` on rank 8), while native communicator residency varied substantially across sampled healthy ranks. The clean result may reflect better unprofiled scheduling or two-sample noise, so EDP-CGA1 advances to the 14-stage workload but is not yet accepted.
- Correctness remains covered by restored-order checksum `2396111`; CGA cluster size changes NCCL scheduling only and does not alter payloads, participants, or collective order. The 14-stage acceptance gate requires close clean parity, no broad native model-EP/dense-DP inflation, and lower exposed reshard than the prior `88.35%` split-Gather result.
- The `88.35%` split-Gather result is not a new 14-stage best: it improves on the `84.97%` host-progress variant by `3.38` percentage points, but trails the prior ordered-path best of `88.90%` by `0.55` percentage points. It remains a useful trace reference because it clears the native-collective interference gate.
- Submitted four-node/segment-4 14-stage EDP-CGA1 acceptance job `2401205` with exact pattern `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E`, healthy EP8/DP2 and NEP EP8/EP4 cases in one allocation, local CUDA graphs, rank-0/4/8 profiler traces on steps 3-4, and clean timing on steps 6-7. At submission it is pending with `StartTime=Unknown` because the GB200 inventory is reserved for maintenance; `gb200` and `gb200-backfill` currently expose the same unavailable nodes, so no duplicate priority job was submitted. The target is at least `95%` clean 14-stage parity while retaining correctness and the native-NCCL gate.

Job `2401205` therefore had no legitimate allocation path and was canceled before running.

No further duplicate resubmission will be used; `2401387` is the sole live experiment.
- Event-level analysis of 14-stage split-Gather trace `2393914` isolates repeatable participant-arrival residency in the first of each pair of equal-size bucket kernels. On follower rank 4, the first Scatter kernel at every MoE boundary takes about `3.15-4.62 ms` versus `0.46-0.59 ms` on owner rank 0, while each second Scatter kernel is about `0.61-0.74 ms`. On reduced owner rank 8, each first EDP kernel takes about `1.69-5.16 ms` versus about `1.35-1.43 ms` on rank 0, while second EDP kernels match near `1.17-1.34 ms`.
- Across six MoE boundaries and two profiled steps, first-kernel excess is about `19.6 ms/step` for follower Scatter plus `8.6 ms/step` for reduced-owner EDP, or roughly `28 ms/step`. This exceeds the measured `17.54 ms` end-to-end gap and makes device-complete phase alignment the next implementation target if EDP-CGA1 is below `95%`: wait for Gather/EDP readiness off the autograd thread, but submit every NCCL collective only from deterministic backward hooks after readiness, never from a worker or behind a future CUDA stream gate.
- Reanalysis of prior synchronous device-aligned 14-stage job `2391000` confirms that device alignment removes most early resident transfer waiting: rank-4 transfer residency fell from split-Gather's `77.65 ms` to `35.59 ms` over two steps, and first transfer kernels fell near `0.79 ms` instead of `3-4.6 ms`. The performance loss was host serialization, not remaining transfer kernels: rank 4 spent `49.16 ms` in `nep_split_wait_scatter_launch`, while ranks 0/8 each spent about `34.5 ms` synchronizing EDP device completion. This supports an asynchronous readiness worker that performs only CUDA-event waits and Gloo rendezvous; deterministic autograd hooks must remain the sole NCCL submitters.
- Main-partition GB300/NVL72 replacement `2401428` started immediately on `theia[0077-0078,0080-0081]` and completed both 14-stage cases `0:0` in `7m21s` with local CUDA graphs. Healthy clean steps 6-7 averaged `131.65 ms`; EDP-CGA1 NEP averaged `155.00 ms`, only `84.94%` latency parity. Every measured iteration was finite with zero skips/NaNs. This is an interim implementation result on NeMo 26.06/NCCL 2.30.4, not the unavailable GB200 acceptance measurement.
- GB300 traces reproduce the GB200 phase-arrival problem. Rank-4 first Scatter kernels average `3.722 ms` versus `0.633 ms` for the second kernel in each pair; rank-8 first EDP kernels average `2.031 ms` versus `1.232 ms` for second kernels. Rank-4 Scatter totals `52.25 ms` over two steps with only `7.5%` direct compute overlap. EDP-CGA1 therefore does not scale beyond the four-stage proxy, and implementation work proceeds with worker-assisted device readiness plus autograd-thread-only NCCL submission.

## 2026-07-15 - Post-graph device-aligned phase scheduler

- The 14-stage `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E` result establishes the new optimization target: ordered-split EP8/EP4 reaches `88.90%` clean per-GPU throughput parity, with a `17.54 ms` healthy-to-NEP gap. Native model-EP and dense-DP collectives are not systematically inflated; trace-matched early participant residency instead adds `16.0-17.4 ms/step` on follower Scatter and `7.0-12.4 ms/step` on selected reduced-owner EDP calls.
- Implemented an opt-in `MEGATRON_NONUNIFORM_EP_POST_GRAPH_PHASES=1` scheduler on top of split host phases. A non-MoE local CUDA graph post-replay hook runs only after useful backward compute has been queued, waits local Gather device completion before the EDP-owner Gloo rendezvous, records and waits EDP device completion before the owner/follower Scatter rendezvous, then launches Scatter. All ProcessGroup calls remain on the autograd thread and the existing completion event is still consumed before the next model-EP collective.
- Added unique non-MoE graph-manager selection so MoE-containing graph replays cannot advance the prior layer pipeline. Added focused tests for graph-manager filtering, Gather/EDP device-aligned call order, and preservation of the model-EP completion fence. Local Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass; the login node has no `uv`.
- Submitted one-node, preflight-only NeMo 25.09 job `2389846` to `gb200-backfill` for Black check, compile, the full focused NEP unit file, and nonblocking slot validation. It is pending for priority with `StartTime=Unknown`; monitoring uses a five-minute cadence.
- The first scheduled poll reported that backfill nodes are reserved for higher-priority work. Submitted identical `gb200` fallback `2389858`; it allocated `lyris0174` at `14:09:04 PDT`, and pending duplicate `2389846` was canceled. The active job initially spent its allocation importing the named NeMo 25.09 image on that node.
- Preflight `2389858` imported NeMo 25.09 and failed before pytest only because Black reported the implementation and test file needed formatting. Added an independently optional `RUN_ISORT` preflight control; no imports changed, so same-node retry `2389878` enabled Black mutation with `RUN_ISORT=0`.
- Retry `2389878` completed `0:0` in `1m37s`: Black converged, all seven checked files passed formatting, compilation and the nonblocking slot test passed, and all 57 focused NEP tests passed in `1.56s`. This clears the unit gate for the small distributed checksum run.
- Parameterized the representative a3b A/B wrapper with a split-case-only post-graph flag plus opt-in profile, per-rank checksum, and selected-rank debug controls. Defaults remain unchanged. An initial attempt to add the flag to the old all-MoE GPT checksum wrapper was removed after auditing graph ownership: it has no non-MoE graph manager and therefore cannot exercise this callback.
- Submitted three-node/segment-3 checksum job `2389893` to `gb200-backfill`. It runs stable and post-graph EP8/EP4 sequentially with the real two-stage `ME` hybrid pattern, four iterations, MBS1/GBS6, TP2, 128 experts, local Mamba/router graphs, profiler off, and per-rank gradient fingerprints. Selected-rank debug must show `device_aligned=True`.

The final iteration writes name-weighted sum, absolute sum, squared sum, weighted coverage, and raw coverage per rank without adding any collective; stable and new cases have identical topology/rank placement, so their corresponding files can be compared directly. Local compilation and diff checks pass.

- No fingerprint file was emitted because Megatron passes zero-based iteration values into `train_step`, while the first hook checked equality with one-based `train_iters`. Changed the opt-in hook to log when `iteration >= train_iters - 1`; local compile/diff checks pass.

- Stable and post-graph gradient coverage matches exactly on every rank. The signed weighted sum is cancellation-sensitive, so it is normalized by weighted absolute mass: maximum rank deltas are `7.586e-8` for signed mass, `1.291e-7` for weighted absolute mass, and `2.738e-7` for weighted squared mass. All pass the `1e-6` correctness gate. Losses also match exactly with zero skips/NaNs.

- Runtime gates remain incremental: first this EP8/EP4 stable-versus-new-path checksum comparison, then a four- or seven-stage profiled EP8/EP4 run that must show no model-EP/dense-DP inflation, and only then the 14-stage healthy/NEP acceptance benchmark.

## 2026-07-15 - Two-repeat original-pattern scaling benchmark

- Added `run_lyris_a3b_ep8_ep4_repeat14_ab.sh` to test whether exposed reshard becomes a measurable end-to-end cost when the exact original `MEMEM*E` structure is repeated twice. The 14-stage `MEMEM*EMEMEM*E` pattern contains six Mamba, six MoE, and two attention stages.
- The A/B keeps the seven-stage experiment's four-node segment-4 allocation, NeMo 26.06 image, TP2, MBS1, 128 experts, sequence length 8192, forced load balancing, GBS8 healthy EP8/EP8, GBS6 ordered-split EP8/EP4, ten iterations, and rank-0/4/8 profile window. Only model depth and the corresponding case timeout change.
- Committed and pushed the fixed experiment definition as `30b6327ae`, then submitted backfill job `2389582`. It is pending for priority with `StartTime=Unknown`; monitoring uses a five-minute cadence and an identical `gb200` fallback will be used if backfill remains unscheduled.
- At the first five-minute poll, `2389582` reported that the required segment-4 nodes were reserved for a higher-priority partition. Submitted identical `gb200` fallback `2389611`; both jobs currently have `StartTime=Unknown`, and the duplicate will be canceled as soon as one allocates.
- Fallback `2389611` allocated `lyris[0039-0041,0051]` at `13:31:05 PDT`; canceled pending duplicate `2389582` at the next five-minute poll. Job `2389611` completed `0:0` in `5m30s`, with healthy and NEP distributed steps completing in `2m57s` and `2m12s`.
- Both cases completed ten finite iterations with zero skipped or NaN iterations. Clean iterations 6-10 averaged `140.48 ms`, `219.48 TFLOP/s/GPU`, and `3.559 samples/GPU/s` for healthy, versus `158.02 ms`, `195.00 TFLOP/s/GPU`, and `3.164 samples/GPU/s` for NEP. Two-repeat NEP therefore reaches `88.90%` per-GPU sample-throughput parity (`88.85%` by reported TFLOP/s/GPU); the `17.54 ms` gap is well above the `3.74/1.98 ms` within-case standard deviations.
- Relative to the seven-stage result, the added repeat costs healthy `47.18 ms` but NEP `64.22 ms`, an incremental NEP penalty of `17.04 ms`. This changes clean parity from `99.47%` at seven stages to `88.90%` at 14 stages and confirms that the short workload had hidden the accumulating exposed cost in rank-level slack.
- Trace residency scales with the six MoE boundaries: rank-0 owner transfer grows from `5.72` to `11.94 ms/step`, rank-4 owner transfer from `15.31` to `30.09 ms/step`, rank-0 EDP from `7.97` to `15.49 ms/step`, and rank-8 EDP from `11.96` to `25.02 ms/step`. Useful non-NCCL compute grows only about `1.5-1.7x`, and reshard overlap remains low: owner transfer `5.4-9.6%`, EDP `0.8-9.0%`, with exactly `0 ms` overlap against the two captured attention-backward graphs.
- Device-arrival skew also scales nearly linearly. The twelve Scatter collectives per step give follower rank 4 about `16.0-17.4 ms/step` more NCCL residency than owner rank 0 while matched operations finish within `0.04 ms`; selected reduced-owner EDP calls add `7.0-12.4 ms/step` of waiting residency while finishing within `0.03 ms` of rank 0. Native model-EP and dense-DP residency are not systematically inflated relative to healthy, so the clean regression is exposed NEP reshard/arrival skew rather than a return of the earlier broad Megatron-collective stall.
- Profiled iterations average `197.55 ms` healthy and `209.75 ms` NEP (`94.18%` parity), but the clean five-iteration window is more stable and shows the production impact. Run artifacts are under `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_ep4_repeat14_ab`; traces exist for ranks 0, 4, and 8 in both cases.

## 2026-07-15 - Original-repeat attention overlap benchmark

- Selected the first exact repeat from the original a3b architecture, `MEMEM*E`, rather than a synthetic attention-heavy pattern. It contains three Mamba, three MoE, and one attention stage, preserving the original ordering and local repeat ratio while increasing reshard boundaries from two to three.
- Parameterized the existing four-layer A/B wrapper without changing its defaults and added `run_lyris_a3b_ep8_ep4_repeat7_ab.sh`. The dedicated job runs healthy EP8/EP8 on 16 GPUs at GBS8 followed by ordered-split EP8/EP4 on 12 GPUs at GBS6 in the same four-node/segment-4 allocation. Both use TP2, MBS1, 128 experts, sequence length 8192, and one sample per data lane.
- The benchmark runs ten iterations per case, profiles steps 3-4 on ranks 0/4/8, leaves step 5 for trace export, and uses steps 6-10 for clean timing. The analysis will attribute the healthy-to-NEP gap to owner-transfer, EDP, model-EP, dense-DP, and non-NCCL compute, including direct reshard/compute concurrency.
- Submitted job `2389331` to `gb200-backfill` for four nodes in segment 4 with a 20-minute allocation limit and eight-minute per-case watchdogs. Its initial state is pending for priority with `StartTime=Unknown`; monitoring uses an exact five-minute cadence.
- Backfill job `2389331` remained unavailable because segment-4 nodes were reserved for the higher-priority partition. Submitted identical priority fallback `2389371`; it allocated immediately on `lyris[0037-0040]` at `12:55:13 PDT`. Canceled pending duplicate `2389331` and confirmed the healthy case started at `12:55:32 PDT` with no initial stderr.
- Priority job `2389371` completed `0:0` in `5m19s`; the healthy and ordered-split steps completed in `2m52s` and `2m08s`. Both cases finished ten finite iterations with zero skipped or NaN iterations and produced rank-0/4/8 traces under `slurm_runs/lyris_a3b_ep8_ep4_repeat7_ab`.
- Clean iterations 6-10 averaged `93.30 ms`, `212.0 TFLOP/s/GPU`, and `5.359 samples/GPU/s` for healthy EP8/EP8, versus `93.80 ms`, `210.8 TFLOP/s/GPU`, and `5.331 samples/GPU/s` for ordered-split EP8/EP4. NEP therefore reached `99.47%` of healthy per-GPU sample throughput (`99.43%` by reported TFLOP/s/GPU); the `0.5 ms` mean difference is below the approximately `4.1 ms` within-case iteration standard deviation.
- Profiled iterations 3-4 expose a residual that clean timing does not resolve: healthy averaged `126.50 ms` and NEP `134.35 ms`, or `94.16%` latency parity while profiling. Non-NCCL kernel time remained similar on sampled ranks (`37-42 ms` over two steps), so model math is not slower.
- Reshard is not being hidden by the inserted attention stage. Across both profiled steps, owner-transfer NCCL overlapped non-NCCL kernels by only `5.6%` on owner rank 0 and `8.4%` on follower rank 4; EDP overlapped by `5.0%` on rank 0 and `0.6%` on reduced owner rank 8. The complete captured attention-backward graph contributed about `2.5 ms/step` of non-NCCL GPU work and had exactly `0 ms` direct overlap with either owner transfer or EDP.
- Matched-rank timelines show participant-arrival skew rather than transfer bandwidth as the dominant residual. For all three Scatters per step, follower rank 4 entered the NCCL kernel `2.25-3.64 ms` before owner rank 0 but finished within `0.06 ms`; this adds about `8.0-8.6 ms/step` of follower NCCL residency. Reduced owner rank 8 similarly entered selected EDP all-reduces up to `3.94 ms` before rank 0 and finished within `0.05 ms`, adding `2.35-5.64 ms/step` of waiting residency.
- The ordering follows the split scheduler implementation: the MoE post-hook enqueues Gather, but EDP and Scatter are host-submitted only from the next `_wait_for_nep_dispatch_launch()` boundary. The Gloo barriers order host submission but do not device-gate the early follower/reduced-owner stream while the owner still has EDP work queued. Attention is therefore too short and too early to hide the late phases; early NCCL participants consume SMs while waiting. Dense DP was not inflated in this run, and the earlier broad Megatron-collective stall remains fixed, although model-EP residency still varies by several milliseconds under profiling.
- This seven-stage experiment reaches clean timing parity through amortization and role-level slack, not close-to-complete reshard overlap. The remaining scale risk is the fixable device-arrival skew on Scatter and EDP, followed by the intentionally late EDP/Scatter launch point; a full 52-layer run could accumulate these exposed per-MoE costs even though this short run is within noise.

## 2026-07-15 - Split host-phase scheduler implementation

- Started the requested EP8/EP4, 2-4 layer debugging cycle. The stable pair-host path remains the default; the experiment is opt-in through `MEGATRON_NONUNIFORM_EP_SPLIT_HOST_PHASES=1` with device and same-communicator readiness gates disabled.
- Split the ProcessGroup dispatch boundary into two ordered host phases. The MoE gradient-ready callback enqueues Gather and asynchronously submits the owner-transfer and EDP-owner Gloo rendezvous, then returns to autograd. The next model-EP boundary waits those already-submitted rendezvous, enqueues EDP and the pre-Scatter pair rendezvous, enqueues Scatter, records completion, and inserts the existing CUDA event dependency before model-EP work.
- Added explicit DDP state for a Gather-launched-but-not-yet-scattered boundary. Expert buckets are no longer required to report ready until the deferred EDP/Scatter phase completes; completion is then checked before the event is recorded. The split path falls back to the stable inline path when task batching would reuse a communication-buffer slot.
- Added focused tests that require exactly one Gather, EDP, and Scatter in order and prove that DDP cannot record/consume the completion event before Scatter. Local Python compilation and `git diff --check` pass. Login-node `pytest` is unavailable, so the mandatory container preflight is the next gate.
- Correctness policy for the GPU cycle: first run two layers at TP2 EP8/EP4, verify finite training plus post-sync gradient parity against a healthy EP8/DP2 run at the same GBS, and only then compare stable versus split scheduling with four layers and profiler traces.

The unchanged healthy EP8/DP2 reference from job `2387931` averaged `73.70 ms`, so ordered split reached `98.4%` of healthy per-GPU throughput and was `1.25%` faster than stable on the same allocation.
- All cases produced finite losses with zero skipped or NaN iterations. Together with job `2388060`, this establishes exact two-layer gradient equivalence plus four-layer performance parity for the ordered split scheduler.
- Trace attribution confirms that the causal Gather-before-EDP fix removed the broad Megatron-collective interference. Reduced owner rank 8 EDP residency fell from about `9.8 ms/step` in the buggy split run to `7.5 ms/step`; model-EP and dense-DP residency no longer show the prior inflation, and clean end-to-end timing is within `1.6%` of healthy.
- A localized residual remains on one MoE boundary: rank 8 can exit the asynchronous Gloo EDP rendezvous and host-submit its first EDP reduction about `2.0 ms` before rank 0, so that reduction has little useful compute overlap. The older stream-memory readiness gate can reduce launch skew, but its per-task form previously hung at larger bucket-group counts. Since the residual does not produce a material throughput gap here, the validated ordered split path remains gate-off rather than reintroducing that scale risk.

## 2026-07-15 - Representative layer-level slowdown attribution

- Compared healthy job `2322588` rank-0 trace with pair-host NEP job `2333379` ranks 0/4/8. The profiled steps averaged `426.48 ms` healthy and `658.67 ms` NEP (`1.54x`); this is larger than the clean iteration ratio (`463.85 / 322.75 = 1.44x`), so the trace is used for attribution rather than the final throughput ratio.
- Reconstructed the 23 MoE layers from the 46 grouped-linear backward events per step. All 22 complete MoE-to-MoE backward windows were slower under NEP: median ratio `2.07x`, range `1.72-2.51x`, and median additive cost `8.50 ms`. The four depth quartiles were `2.03x`, `2.09x`, `2.01x`, and `2.08x`, showing that the slowdown is distributed uniformly through model depth rather than concentrated in a few layers. These windows account for `185.18 ms` of the `232.19 ms` profiled-step delta (about `80%`).
- CUDA graph execution itself was effectively unchanged: aligned median NEP/healthy execution-span ratios were `1.005x` for Mamba and `1.001x` for attention; router graph kernel time was also `1.005x` at the median. The delay is inserted around/between graph execution: median launch-to-first-kernel delay grew `1.81x` for Mamba and `4.40x` for attention. Owner rank 0, follower rank 4, and reduced-owner rank 8 had nearly identical total step spans and MoE-window medians, so the regression is a synchronized per-MoE-boundary communication/scheduling cost, not slower layer math or one localized straggler layer.
- Code/trace correlation clarifies that the extra existing-Megatron NCCL time is primarily scheduler serialization and participant-arrival skew, not demonstrated raw bandwidth interference. Each layer Gather, EDP reduction, and Scatter are enqueued serially on one shared NEP dispatch stream; `_wait_for_nep_dispatch_launch()` then makes the compute/current stream wait on the layer completion event before the next model-EP boundary. The overlapping participant sets differ (`nep_owner_transfer` pair `[0,4]`, `ep_dp` pair `[0,8]`, regular EP group `[0-7]`), so pair/owner barriers align local phases but can deliver the four pairs to the next full EP collective at different times. An early EP NCCL kernel then records long residency while waiting for the other EP ranks.
- Dense-DP overlap is explicitly disabled for the NCCL NEP path: parameter hooks record dense readiness without calling the normal Megatron `start_grad_sync`, and `_start_delayed_dense_grad_syncs()` launches all dense buckets only from `finish_grad_sync()` after the NEP drain. Its extra tail is therefore an implementation constraint, not evidence that reshard traffic made the same dense all-reduce intrinsically slower. `NCCL_LAUNCH_ORDER_IMPLICIT=1` may add cross-communicator ordering pressure, but the current evidence does not isolate it as the dominant performance cause; CUDA connection count is not causal on Blackwell.

## 2026-07-09 - Stream-ordered EDP readiness parity recovery

- Reaffirmed that correctness alone is insufficient: no further scale-up is acceptable until the matched a3b EP8/4 NEP run is close to healthy job `2322588` (`322.75 ms`, `282.75 TFLOP/s/GPU`).
- ProcessGroup zero-CTA readiness smoke `2327976` hung at the first readiness/EDP sequence and was canceled after `4m33s`; zero-CTA ProcessGroup AllGather is not viable on this stack.
- CUDA stream-memory probe `2328305` passed on the exact NeMo 26.06 / GB200 stack after warming CUDA before the wait. `cuStreamWaitValue32` and `cuStreamWriteValue32` can therefore provide a zero-SM, stream-ordered readiness release.
- Repository inspection found the intended replacement had only partially landed: `_cuda_stream_ops.py` was present, but the active gate still used the failed ProcessGroup AllGather while runtime group creation left it disabled. The passing fallback consequently launched EDP without a non-SM readiness gate and retained the known early-owner contention/performance loss.
- Replaced the per-task gate with an async EDP Gloo rendezvous serviced by one ordered host worker. Each local rendezvous completion writes a generation token on a CUDA signal stream; the existing NEP communication stream waits on that token before launching Megatron's normal ProcessGroup EDP all-reduce. This preserves task order without blocking autograd or parking an NCCL kernel on SMs.
- Added focused lifecycle/error-drain coverage, changed the hardware probe to exercise the shared helper, and included both new files in mandatory isort/Black/compile preflight. Next gates are container preflight, TP2 EP8/4 smoke/profile, then the exact matched a3b EP8/4 benchmark.

The saved device time was converted into phase waiting: full-rank Gloo barrier duration rose from `~88-93 ms` to `~199-320 ms`, while reduced owner rank 8 spent only `~8 ms`, proving the full-EP fences synchronize unrelated owner/follower pairs and preserve the slowest-pair critical path.
- Selected a pair-scoped ordered pipeline. Each disjoint owner/follower pair will fence Gather and EDP host submission only within its transfer Gloo group. The existing CUDA stream-memory gate will be extended so its worker waits for the local Gather CUDA event before entering the owner EDP rendezvous; a second gate will hold Scatter until the owner's EDP CUDA event completes. Background threads perform only CUDA-event waits and Gloo signaling; all NCCL host calls remain on the autograd thread.
- Implemented pair-scoped host ordering plus device-ready EDP/Scatter stream gates. The worker now waits its supplied CUDA event before launching the relevant Gloo rendezvous and writing the stream generation token. The inline scheduler uses owner-transfer Gloo groups before EDP and Scatter host submission; it no longer fences unrelated pairs through the physical-EP Gloo group.
- Pair-gate preflight `2331337` completed `0:0` in `1m41s`. Required formatting and compile checks passed, all 48 focused tests passed in `1.12s`, and the nonblocking slot test passed. Runtime validation restarts at four layers.
- Four-layer pair-gate job `2331385` completed finite iteration 1 (`10.219 s`) but hung in iteration 2 and timed out at `6m24s`. Group-2 markers localized the cycle: rank 8 completed its local event and entered the EDP Gloo wait, while rank 0 remained on the Gather CUDA event and rank 4 remained on the Scatter CUDA event. Pair Gather/EDP host barriers themselves balanced.
- The reduced owner had no transfer-group barrier and could host-submit EDP before the full owner finished Gather host ordering. A stream-memory wait controls device execution but does not change ProcessGroup/NCCL host launch order. The fix adds one synchronous EDP-owner Gloo barrier after the full owner/follower Gather barrier and before EDP host submission; the later device-ready EDP gate remains responsible for avoiding an early resident all-reduce.
- EDP-host-order preflight `2331534` completed `0:0` in `1m53s`; all 48 focused tests, formatting/compile checks, and the nonblocking slot test passed. The four-layer runtime gate will be retried before any larger job.
- Corrected four-layer job `2331581` again timed out after finite iteration 1. Gather, EDP-owner-launch, and pre-Scatter host barriers all balanced, but rank 0 remained on group-6 Gather device completion while rank 8 waited in the matching EDP readiness Gloo call and rank 4 waited on Scatter readiness. This leaves NCCL implicit cross-communicator launch ordering as the active cycle, not unmatched host participants.
- The next controlled run changes only `NCCL_LAUNCH_ORDER_IMPLICIT=0`. Unlike the earlier failed flag-off job `2329424`, the current topology has disjoint transfer communicators plus explicit pair host ordering, Gather-complete EDP gates, and EDP-complete Scatter gates; these dependencies should be sufficient without NCCL imposing one implicit order across unequal communicator memberships.
- Flag-off job `2331726` reproduced the exact group-6 timeout and unmatched event markers from `2331581`. The cycle is therefore caused by host-submitting future ProcessGroup collectives behind stream-memory waits, not by NCCL's implicit-order setting.
- The next isolation keeps disjoint owner/follower groups and all pairwise Gather/EDP/Scatter host-order barriers but sets `MEGATRON_NONUNIFORM_EP_EDP_READY_GATE=0`. If it passes, the pair barriers can be benchmarked independently; device-completion overlap must then be redesigned without pre-submitting blocked ProcessGroup work.
- Gate-disabled pair-host job `2331883` completed `0:0` in `1m51s`; both iterations were finite (`10.221 s`, `5.153 s`) with zero skips/NaNs. Ranks 0/4/8 balanced every Gather, EDP-launch, and pre-Scatter pair barrier, all 16 pre-sync drains exited, and no stream-readiness marker ran. Pair-scoped host ordering is correct; validation advances to six and eight layers before representative profiling.
- Six-layer job `2331998` remained pending for hours and missed multiple test-only windows; it was moved in place to `gb200-backfill`, then replaced by identical fresh request `2333078` when the scheduler still did not allocate it. Superseded `2331998` was canceled before running.
- Six-layer replacement `2333078` completed `0:0` in `1m49s`. Iterations were finite at `9.991 s` and `5.053 s`, with zero skips/NaNs; ranks 0/4/8 balanced all six pair barriers, all 24 pre-sync drains, and six ordered completions. Validation advances to eight layers.
- Eight-layer gate-disabled pair-host job `2333329` completed `0:0` in `3m28s`. Iterations were finite at `9.764 s` and `4.955 s`, with zero skips/NaNs; ranks 0/4/8 balanced all eight pair barriers, all 32 pre-sync drains, and eight ordered completions. The staged correctness ladder is complete; the exact a3b profile/benchmark is next.
- Representative pair-host job `2333379` completed correctly at `463.85 ms` and `196.75 TFLOP/s/GPU` over clean iterations 6-7. This is a `12.8%` gain over fanout/global-fence job `2331117`, but only `69.6%` of healthy job `2322588`, so parity remains unresolved.

The remaining profile gap is GPU-side: rank 0 has `~65 ms` exposed owner transfer, `~53 ms` exposed EDP, `~58 ms` extra model-EP residency, and `~25 ms` extra dense-DP residency relative to healthy.
- NEP performs 46 per-context EDP all-reduces per step while healthy Megatron emits 13 coalesced expert-DP reductions. The next change coalesces the two owner contexts ready at each of the 23 MoE boundaries into one native coalesced all-reduce, reducing NEP EDP launches from 46 to 23 without changing tensor contents or replica membership.
- Implemented batch-level owner EDP reduction with `torch.distributed.all_reduce_coalesced`. It validates owner contexts share one EDP group and async mode, records the shared Work against each original buffer slot, and falls back to per-context reduction for singleton batches or the experimental device-gate path.

Iterations were `10.971 s` and `5.534 s`; the second iteration was slower than pre-coalescing job `2331883` (`5.153 s`), so only the representative profile can establish performance value. Correctness validation advances to six and eight layers.
- Six-layer coalesced-EDP job `2333604` completed `0:0` with six coalesced steady reductions on both owner replicas, no steady per-context fallback, balanced drains, and finite iterations (`9.934 s`, `5.024 s`). Its second iteration is effectively flat versus pre-coalescing job `2333078` (`5.053 s`), so validation advances to eight layers but no performance benefit is claimed.

- Rejected the blocking task-order gate despite its unit-test correctness. It would serialize backward progress at every owner task and therefore codify the loss of parity rather than solve it.
- Reconnected the existing nonblocking host phase scheduler to the ProcessGroup path. A ready task now launches Gather and records a CUDA completion event; asynchronous source/EDP Gloo rendezvous admit the owner EDP reduction only after both replica gathers are complete; asynchronous source rendezvous admit Scatter only after EDP completion. The autograd thread polls these stages from later grad hooks and blocks only at the final forced drain.
- Async phase-gate preflight `2327669` completed `0:0` in `2m00s` with all 44 focused tests passing. Rank traces showed effectively zero NCCL/non-NCCL overlap.
- Root cause: local CUDA graph replay does not provide enough Python grad-hook polling to advance the pending host state machine. One task launched during backward; the remaining 31 tasks were force-drained serially in `finish_grad_sync`. This design is rejected for performance.
- Selected a stream-ordered zero-SM readiness gate. The dedicated EDP readiness communicator uses NCCL's zero-CTA policy and registered persistent 4-byte buffers; each owner enqueues native `ncclGather` immediately before its EDP all-reduce. Early owners therefore wait without a resident SM kernel, while CUDA stream order launches EDP as soon as the peer arrives and requires no Python polling.
- Zero-SM native-readiness preflight `2327782` passed all 44 tests. TP2 smoke `2327784` initialized the registered readiness buffers but stalled before logging iteration 1 and was canceled after `5m41s` rather than consuming the full allocation.
- Debug ordering identified the bug: reduced owner rank 8 enqueued EDP groups 2-7 while full owner rank 0 was still interleaving earlier owner Gather/Scatter phases. Direct ctypes NCCL Gather bypassed ProcessGroupNCCL launch-order bookkeeping, reintroducing the cross-communicator cycle.
- Replaced direct native readiness with ProcessGroup `all_gather_into_tensor` on the same zero-CTA, registered-buffer communicator. This keeps the readiness operation copy-engine eligible while restoring ProcessGroup launch ordering and Work-based stream dependencies. Added a blocking full TP-lane Gloo task barrier only for the excluded first warmup batch so first-batch communicator ordering cannot race.
- ProcessGroup zero-SM preflight `2327843` failed only in three focused fixtures: one missing async-handle storage, and two missing `is_first_batch`/DP-CP Gloo fields after first-batch fencing was introduced. Dependent smoke `2327845` was canceled by dependency without allocation.
- Corrected the fixtures and strengthened excluded first-batch ordering to wait for device completion and rendezvous the full TP lane after Gather, EDP, and Scatter. Steady-state measured iterations remain fully asynchronous and do not use these blocking warmup fences.
- Corrected preflight `2327931` reduced the failures to one useful slot-reuse assertion. It showed the zero-SM readiness buffer was reused without ordering the new AllGather behind the prior Work. Added `_order_nep_nccl_buffer_slot` before each readiness launch; dependent smoke `2327935` was canceled by dependency without allocation.
